- 2026-11-17 11:08 by w-743-opus · asked a-3 (stuck) · 741's line asks that a lone `o is Config`, where Config is a value the file declares, give no edge. C# reads that form the other way: `o is Config` with no designation is the is-type operator, which binds Config as a type first and takes it as a constant only when no type of that name is found (Roslyn's parser turns such a constant pattern into an is-type expression, and its binder tries the type first, for compatibility with C# 6, where a value never hid a type there). So in `class C { const int Config = 1; bool A(object o) => o is Config; }` beside `using Lib;` with a type Lib.Config, C# tests the type Lib.Config: the edge the reader gives today is true, and the line would drop it. The value hides the type only where C# binds the pattern's expression first: a switch arm `Config =>`, `case Config:`, `o is not Config`, an `or` or `and` pattern, a parenthesized one and a positional subpattern (750 already reads that last place so). At 0995b7c all three of `o is Config`, `Config =>` and `o is not Config` read Config as a type. Options: (a) the line as written: every lone constant-pattern name the file declares as a value gives no edge, `o is Config` included; cost: a true edge lost wherever a file has a value and a type of one name, a false green. (b) the C# reading: `Config =>`, `case Config:` and `o is not Config` with Config a value the file declares give no edge, while `o is Config` and each test of a `||` chain keep the edge to the type; the line changes to say so. (c) no change; the false edge of `Config =>` beside a constant of that name stays. Recommended: (b). 741 stays uncommitted until this is answered; 747 goes on.

- 2026-11-17 11:28 key 20261117T1032-grqq by w-743-opus · 747 evidence · Assumed: the type is read only where the member access is compared with (==, !=) or assigned to the bare value itself, for a value of a written type of its own name as for a var local or a lambda parameter. Read anywhere, the typed form gave 114 new corpus references, among them instance members C# reads through the value (Season.ProviderIds, DeviceProfile.SubtitleProfiles, User.Id, JellyfinDbContext.Dispose(), TranscodeSeekInfo.ToString()) and Video3DFormat.HasValue on a nullable; the narrow form gives 49, all enum members read through the type. It also keeps the existing case csharp-local-shadows-type-name-silence (var OrderStatus = o; OrderStatus.ToString()) and the lambda shadow unit test, which a var reading everywhere breaks. A written Status? is not read so, since of a value type it is Nullable<Status>. Lost by this choice: Status.Active beside such a value in a return, an argument, an attribute argument or a tuple (about 65 jellyfin references), where the file already has the edge on the value's declaration.
- 2026-11-17 11:28 key 20261117T1032-nvgi by w-743-opus · 743 evidence · Assumed: a commit of its own follows the fix (659e2c1): the first form climbed from the constant pattern through each negated pattern with .parent, which the extractors' ancestor guard refuses; npm run check found it, and the walk now carries the test down instead. The output is the same, so no rev moves.
- 2026-11-17 11:28 key 20261117T1032-grqq by w-743-opus · 747 body · changelog · was (none) · by w-743-opus
- 2026-11-17 11:28 key 20261117T1032-twkd by w-743-opus · 750 evidence · Assumed: a simple name in a positional pattern gives no edge where the file declares a value of that name anywhere (the file-wide test the receiver reading uses), as the line says. C# finds the value only where it is in scope, and a value in scope there compiles only as a constant, so a value of that name in another member drops a true edge; a dotted element is read as the type it spells, first name first, as a lone dotted constant pattern is.

- 2026-11-17 12:04 key 20261117T1032-imsh by rev-743-opus · 741 review approve · by rev-743-opus (fresh) · model claude-opus-5-5@xhigh · batch 20261117T1204-sirp · Approve: no Critical or Important finding; two Minor. Read the five commits 845cb0e, 0995b7c, 42749f4, 659e2c1, 619bccf against base 1f3476b. Each [test] line re-run: the seven new cases on a tree of v1.1.0 (743, 750 and 747 edge cases and the 741 silence case red, the three guards green), green at 619bccf; npm run check on a scratch clone green (2760, 2757 pass, 0 fail, 3 skipped, dist/ fresh); mutations of dist turn each guard red for the right reason (is-type exception, value test in a tuple, any written type, beside-the-value-only, negated climb). Corpus: CleanArchitecture, eShop and jellyfin pass corpus:edges; against the 1f3476b goldens the only moves are the 49 edges of 747 (eShop 14, jellyfin 35, none removed), each read at its source line: an enum member compared with or assigned to a property of that enum's type and name, in a file whose property declaration already has the edge to the same file. The C# readings match the spec and Roslyn: o is Config binds the type first (is-type operator), every other pattern binds the expression first, so a value in scope there is a constant; Color Color (12.8.7.2) reads a static member through the type. Conventions hold: one issue per commit, dist/ with src/, rev 44 to 48 one per output change (none for 659e2c1), catalogue 992 to 999 in the test's three places (README.md:201 and CLAUDE.md:9 are the plan's final pass), no trailer, no existing case or test weakened. 900 truncated or spliced C# files raise no exception. Minor 1 (747): an inferred value (a var local, a foreach var, an implicitly typed lambda parameter) is taken as of the type of its own name, so a PascalCase local of another type compared with its own instance member (var Node = f.Root; Node == Node.Parent, Root a TreeNode) gives an edge to an imported type Node the file does not otherwise use; the code comment on besideItself calls Node = Node.Next the one false reading this allows, and the changelog says the new findings sit mostly in files that already depend on the type, so neither names this one; the plan names the risk and the line asks for the var and lambda edge, so the fix is the wording of the comment and the changelog (a qualified written type of another type with an instance member, Other.Status Status beside using App.Model, is a rarer case of the same). Minor 2 (741): the changelog says C# binds the name to the value there, but the test is file-wide: a value Config in another class of the file also silences o is not Config, Config =>, case Config: and Config or string where C# binds the type (4 edges at v1.1.0, none at 619bccf); the plan and the worker's note name it, the changelog could say the value may be anywhere in the file. (+1 more lines, whole text in the issue's Reviews section)

- 2026-11-17 12:06 key 20261117T1032-svei by w-737-opus · 737 evidence · Fault not reproduced, nothing changed (confirm-the-fault-before-the-fix). At release/1.2.0 (1f3476b) and at 94b0558, the shape of the issue, Cargo.toml [lib] path = "lib.rs" and lib.rs holding only mod outer { #[path = "loaded.rs"] mod x; }, gives the edge outer/loaded.rs:1 -> outer/child.rs through the catalogue runner, on disk and through a listed view. Also tried: the decoy lib/outer/child.rs and lib/outer/loaded.rs, no edition, src/main.rs beside it, the package in a workspace member, path = "./lib.rs", pub items and a pub fn in lib.rs, the inline module on one line, child as outer/child/mod.rs, and mod a; beside it: every shape gives the edge. The case built under rustc 1.99.1. holdsModulesBeside reads fileIsRoot from rustTargetFor, which is true for a [lib] path file, so the declaresModule argument it passes does not decide it. The 1.1.0 known limit (CHANGELOG.md:114) does not describe the code as it is; the final pass may drop it from the 1.2.0 notes or the owner may close 737 as not reproduced.
- 2026-11-17 12:06 key 20261117T1032-zfrj by w-737-opus · 759 evidence · Assumed: a mod with a path attribute inside an inline module, under an inline module with a path attribute, in a function body, in a cfg_if! branch or with a path a cfg_attr applies stays silent: the acceptance names mods outside inline modules, and the declarations side reads no such file either (pathModules).
- 2026-11-17 12:06 key 20261117T1032-zfrj by w-737-opus · 759 evidence · Assumed: the file binds when it exists in a Cargo package, with no exclusion check, as every other Rust path does (the Rust resolver takes no isExcluded); the plan's 'not excluded' is not a test of the line.
- 2026-11-17 12:06 key 20261117T1032-zfrj by w-737-opus · 759 evidence · Assumed: ten existing cases whose fixtures hold a mod with a path attribute among the items of a file gain the edge it gives as a new Expect line (every edge checked against the Reference: the path is read from the directory of the declaring file), and rust-mod-path-attribute-before-doc-comment-silence keeps silence with its Rule rewritten, since its files lie in one node; no existing edge or answer moved. Corpus: ripgrep gains 2 edges (crates/core/index/mod.rs:5 and :8, the cfg'd #[path] mods), explained and committed in the golden as its own commit 8e245bf.
- 2026-11-17 12:06 key 20261117T1032-ovte by w-737-opus · 760 evidence · Assumed: a path in a type (fn t(_d: datacrate::Data)) is read as one in an expression; the acceptance names a function body only, and the type gives the same dependency.
- 2026-11-17 12:06 key 20261117T1032-ovte by w-737-opus · 760 evidence · Assumed: a glob use in any scope on the way to the module (use super::*; in mod tests) silences a bare path there, since it may bring in the name; a capitalised name, Self, a primitive type (u32::MAX), a leading :: and a tool attribute (#[rustfmt::skip]) give no use; an extern crate alias of the scope silences too.
- 2026-11-17 12:06 key 20261117T1032-ovte by w-737-opus · 760 evidence · Assumed: in:: reads the extern prelude in every edition, edition 2015 included, never from the crate root (rustc since 1.30 puts --extern crates in the extern prelude of non-use paths in both editions); a unit test pins the 2015 reading, no catalogue case does.
- 2026-11-17 12:06 key 20261117T1032-ovte by w-737-opus · 760 evidence · Corpus: ripgrep gains 105 edges (into crates/grep, crates/ignore and crates/index, path dependencies of the root package, and ignore's own library from its example) and react 573 (into compiler/crates/react_compiler_*, path dependencies of each package, and react_compiler_ast's own library from its test); none removed; each file and target checked against Cargo.toml and for a local binding of the crate name, explained per file in corpus/explained.md and taken into the goldens. zed parses no Rust file (0 edges before and after); next-intl's Rust edges do not move.

- 2026-11-17 12:25 key 20261117T1032-bhyc by w-765-opus · 188 evidence · For the final pass, the CHANGELOG [1.2.0] "Known limits" line of 188 and 765 together, exactly: "- A file that is not source code in its language parses in time that grows with its size (JavaScript takes 8.6 s for 1 000 000 characters of random text), and a short text repeated over and over parses in memory that grows about fourfold each time the file doubles (TypeScript `a<a,` repeated takes 1.5 GiB at 32 KiB, and C# `int x = f(a < b, a < b, …` makes the runtime abort at its 2 GiB limit at 32 KiB); a consumer caps the size of the files it reads, skips binaries, and passes `progressCallback` to stop a parse past its budget." The numbers are those of README.md's host section on branch jarl/765-parse-stop (web-tree-sitter 0.27.2, the 1.2.0 grammar pins).
- 2026-11-17 12:25 key 20261117T1032-bhyc by w-765-opus · 188 evidence · Assumed: the README half is one paragraph in the host section written for 765 (commit beaa9b6, the time growth, the memory growth, the caps, skipping binaries and the stop), and the 188 commit (124229c) adds only its per-language times; the timing probe is random printable ASCII with 5% newlines from a seeded xorshift, parsed through the host with a guard callback that stops past 120 s or 6 GiB of RSS (it never stopped), each size in its own process under MemoryMax=8G, twice.
- 2026-11-17 12:25 key 20261117T1032-prsu by w-765-opus · 765 evidence · Assumed: the option is named ParseFileOptions with the one field progressCallback (the runtime's name), it is exported as a type marked "—", the callback receives the runtime's ParseState as given, and only a return of exactly true stops (what the runtime itself honours). A callback that throws is caught and turned into a stop, and the call rejects with what it threw: a throw unwinding through the runtime mid-parse would leave the parser in an unknown state and would be taken for a scanner trap and retried. The error code is EPARSESTOPPED, after ELOCKED and ELOCKBREAK.
- 2026-11-17 12:26 key 20261117T1032-prsu by w-765-opus · 765 evidence · Assumed: line 2 holds at v1.1.0 as well (no callback was ever passed), so its red is shown on a mutant build that always passes a no-op progressCallback; the stub-runtime test also checks that a callback does reach the runtime when the option is given, which is red at the failing-test commit.
- 2026-11-17 12:26 key 20261117T1032-prsu by w-765-opus · 765 evidence row (line 532df7a157f0) · probe through the host on dist of beaa9b6 (web-tree-sitter 0.27.2, 1.2.0 grammar pins, Node v24.22.2), one process per size under systemd-run MemoryMax=8G, twice: a<a, (.ts) and int x = f(a < b, ... (.cs) at 8, 16, 32 KiB unstopped; both at 64 KiB stopped past byte 1024; random printable text, 5% newlines, 250 000 and 1 000 000 characters for js, cs, rs, py, java

- 2026-11-17 12:32 key 20261117T1032-svei by rev-737-opus · 737 review approve · by rev-737-opus (fresh) · model claude-opus-5-5@xhigh · batch 20261117T1232-hdnb · Approve at e5885d7, Minor findings only. 737: not reproduced. The issue's shape ([lib] path = "lib.rs", lib.rs holding only mod outer { #[path = "loaded.rs"] mod x; }, mod child; in outer/loaded.rs) gives outer/loaded.rs:1 -> outer/child.rs at 1f3476b, 8d31e57, 94b0558 and e5885d7, on disk and through a listed view, in 16 variants (mod a; beside it, src/lib.rs, decoys under lib/outer/, no edition, a workspace member, child as mod.rs and others), and rustc 1.99.1 loads exactly those files; the only silent reading is a case written without owner= attributes, which is silent for src/lib.rs as well. Its line cannot be met, since nothing is red at v1.1.0, so the worker's missing row is no fault of the work: 737 should be dropped as not reproduced (the jarl decides), and the 1.1.0 known limit at CHANGELOG.md:114 describes no fault, for the final pass to take back. 759 and 760: red at 1f3476b for the right reasons (21 failures, every one a new test or a newly expected edge; the silence and guard tests pass), green at the tip: npm run check 2764 tests, 2761 pass, 0 fail, 3 skipped, dist/ fresh; both build tests ran under rustc 1.99.1 (4 pass, 0 skipped). Corpus verify ok for ripgrep, react, zed and next-intl, 0 added, 0 removed; all 680 new edges checked by script: the crate named on the line is a path dependency of the file's package, or its own library from an example or a test, and no such file binds the name with a mod, an alias or an extern crate; read by hand at their lines in hiargs.rs, search.rs, defs.rs, haystack.rs, zsh.rs, index/mod.rs, pipeline.rs and the napi crate's lib.rs, all true. A mutation that drops any one scope guard (glob, type-namespace names, names in scope, the stop at the module) fails a unit test. docs/api.md names mod:: and in:: with examples. One issue per commit, dist/ with src/, rev 26 to 27 to 28, no agent trailer; against the current release branch only the count and rev lines conflict, for the merger. Findings, all Minor: (1) 760: a module declared in a cfg_if! branch, which Runes reads as declared and gives its mod edge, and a use in such a branch are no names in scope, so a path through them reads the crate of that name: datacrate::dc(1u8) beside cfg_if! { if #[cfg(unix)] { mod datacrate; } } gives src/lib.rs:9 -> datacrate/src/lib.rs where the path dependency's root also declares dc, while rustc 1.99.1 binds src/datacrate.rs (the dependency's dc takes no argument and the crate builds); with no such dependency, imp::g() through a cfg_if! use alias now answers absent outside where 1.1.0 gave no use. Rare, no corpus repository has it; the root cause also reaches use paths since 1.1.0, filed outside. (2) 760: #[diagnostic::on_unimplemented(...)] and #[rust_analyzer::skip] are attribute namespaces outside TOOL_ATTRIBUTES (rustfmt, clippy), so they emit in::diagnostic::on_unimplemented and in::rust_analyzer::skip and answer outside; no edge. (3) 759: mod::<path> binds any path that exists, a directory too (mod::sub binds src/sub through a view and on disk), and a raw identifier path is unrawed into the new forms (use r#mod::x; gives mod::x, as it did at 1.1.0; r#in::b() gives in::in::b); only code rustc refuses or a glob-imported module named r#mod reaches either, so the docs' 'no path of the source starts with it' holds only after unrawing. (4) docs/api.md: the names-in-scope paragraph still says a mod with a path attribute in a cfg_if! branch 'is skipped as elsewhere', which no longer holds among a file's items; the in:: paragraph speaks of expressions and types, while macro paths (log::info!, datacrate::m!) and attribute paths (#[tokio::main]) give in:: uses too, and 'the path as written' is unrawed. (5) corpus/explained.md: the ripgrep lines repeat one template, so hiargs.rs, zsh.rs, defs.rs and lowargs.rs are said to write grep::searcher::BinaryDetection and grep::printer::Stats with no use, and hiargs.rs ignore::DirEntry and ignore::Error; hiargs.rs writes ColorSpecs, MmapChoice and WalkBuilder and has use grep::printer::{ColorSpecs, SummaryKind}; the edges themselves are right. (6) 760: a path's scope walk costs the depth of the blocks around it: 16000 nested blocks with a path each read in 5.8 s at the tip against 0.18 s at 1.1.0; the older half for use declarations is filed outside. Open: none. (+2 more lines, whole text in the issue's Reviews section)

The issue's shape ([lib] path = "lib.rs", lib.rs holding only mod outer { #[path = "loaded.rs"] mod x; }, mod child; in outer/loaded.rs) gives outer/loaded.rs:1 -> outer/child.rs at 1f3476b, 8d31e57, 94b0558 and e5885d7, on disk and through a listed view, in 16 variants (mod a; beside it, src/lib.rs, decoys under lib/outer/, no edition, a workspace member, child as mod.rs and others), and rustc 1.99.1 loads exactly those files; the only silent reading is a case written without owner= attributes, which is silent for src/lib.rs as well. Its line cannot be met, since nothing is red at v1.1.0, so the worker's missing row is no fault of the work: 737 should be dropped as not reproduced (the jarl decides), and the 1.1.0 known limit at CHANGELOG.md:114 describes no fault, for the final pass to take back. 759 and 760: red at 1f3476b for the right reasons (21 failures, every one a new test or a newly expected edge; the silence and guard tests pass), green at the tip: npm run check 2764 tests, 2761 pass, 0 fail, 3 skipped, dist/ fresh; both build tests ran under rustc 1.99.1 (4 pass, 0 skipped). Corpus verify ok for ripgrep, react, zed and next-intl, 0 added, 0 removed; all 680 new edges checked by script: the crate named on the line is a path dependency of the file's package, or its own library from an example or a test, and no such file binds the name with a mod, an alias or an extern crate; read by hand at their lines in hiargs.rs, search.rs, defs.rs, haystack.rs, zsh.rs, index/mod.rs, pipeline.rs and the napi crate's lib.rs, all true. A mutation that drops any one scope guard (glob, type-namespace names, names in scope, the stop at the module) fails a unit test. docs/api.md names mod:: and in:: with examples. One issue per commit, dist/ with src/, rev 26 to 27 to 28, no agent trailer; against the current release branch only the count and rev lines conflict, for the merger. Findings, all Minor: (1) 760: a module declared in a cfg_if! branch, which Runes reads as declared and gives its mod edge, and a use in such a branch are no names in scope, so a path through them reads the crate of that name: datacrate::dc(1u8) beside cfg_if! { if #[cfg(unix)] { mod datacrate; } } gives src/lib.rs:9 -> datacrate/src/lib.rs where the path dependency's root also declares dc, while rustc 1.99.1 binds src/datacrate.rs (the dependency's dc takes no argument and the crate builds); with no such dependency, imp::g() through a cfg_if! use alias now answers absent outside where 1.1.0 gave no use. Rare, no corpus repository has it; the root cause also reaches use paths since 1.1.0, filed outside. (2) 760: #[diagnostic::on_unimplemented(...)] and #[rust_analyzer::skip] are attribute namespaces outside TOOL_ATTRIBUTES (rustfmt, clippy), so they emit in::diagnostic::on_unimplemented and in::rust_analyzer::skip and answer outside; no edge. (3) 759: mod::<path> binds any path that exists, a directory too (mod::sub binds src/sub through a view and on disk), and a raw identifier path is unrawed into the new forms (use r#mod::x; gives mod::x, as it did at 1.1.0; r#in::b() gives in::in::b); only code rustc refuses or a glob-imported module named r#mod reaches either, so the docs' 'no path of the source starts with it' holds only after unrawing. (4) docs/api.md: the names-in-scope paragraph still says a mod with a path attribute in a cfg_if! branch 'is skipped as elsewhere', which no longer holds among a file's items; the in:: paragraph speaks of expressions and types, while macro paths (log::info!, datacrate::m!) and attribute paths (#[tokio::main]) give in:: uses too, and 'the path as written' is unrawed. (5) corpus/explained.md: the ripgrep lines repeat one template, so hiargs.rs, zsh.rs, defs.rs and lowargs.rs are said to write grep::searcher::BinaryDetection and grep::printer::Stats with no use, and hiargs.rs ignore::DirEntry and ignore::Error; hiargs.rs writes ColorSpecs, MmapChoice and WalkBuilder and has use grep::printer::{ColorSpecs, SummaryKind}; the edges themselves are right. (6) 760: a path's scope walk costs the depth of the blocks around it: 16000 nested blocks with a path each read in 5.8 s at the tip against 0.18 s at 1.1.0; the older half for use declarations is filed outside. Open: none. (+2 more lines, whole text in the issue's Reviews section)

- 2026-11-17 12:39 key 20261117T1032-eyqh by w-758-opus · 757 evidence · Assumed: a root named by configuration replaces the src/ rule for its project, as src/ replaces the project's directory (setuptools and Poetry put only the named directories on sys.path); a project whose named roots are all missing keeps the 1.1.x rule. The keys are read by their full TOML key path (so packages.find.where under [tool.setuptools] reads like the table form), and every other spelling is refused: an inline packages = { find = ... }, [[tool.poetry.packages]], a package-dir entry for a named package, a value with a non-string element, an absolute, backslashed or escaping path, a repeated setup.cfg key. setup.cfg where takes its first directory, as setuptools does.

- 2026-11-17 12:42 key 20261117T1032-eiig by rev-762-opus · 756 review changes · by rev-762-opus (fresh) · model claude-opus-5-5@high · batch 20261117T1242-phww · Important (756): the corpus gate is red at the tip. corpus.mjs verify gives jest +73 and next-intl +20 unexplained (both routine tier, so CI's corpus job fails) and react +131 (heavy tier); only skywalking-booster-ui and vitepress were updated. Every added edge sits on a vi.mock or jest.mock line and reads the mock as the issue intends, so the fix is the goldens and corpus/explained.md for jest, next-intl and react, not code. With the v1.1.0 extractor the same verify gives jest and next-intl ok. Minor (762): yes, a regression against v1.1.0, which skipped nothing: a <!-- outside a comment (an attribute value, the raw text of <style> or <textarea>, a Svelte {@html '<!--'}) that a later --> closes, including i-->0 inside the script, hides every script between them, and the component has no view where v1.1.0 read ./a at line 2. It needs a stray <!-- with a --> after the script, and nothing in the corpus has one (256 Vue views identical to v1.1.0, same length and lines). docs/api.md does not name this limit; name it, or skip <!-- inside a tag's quoted attribute values. Minor (762): the changelog says the change only removes uses, but <!-- <script> --> before a real script gave v1.1.0 no use and now gives the real script's, so it can add uses. Minor (762): the test for a commented script beside a real one is Vue only; the same Svelte case passes on the tip. Minor (763): the src value goes into the view unescaped and unfiltered. A protocol-relative //host/a.js is not dropped as https: is, and a root-absolute /js/a.js in <svelte:head> becomes an unfollowed use although it is a browser URL. A src of .\x.js reads as '.' and resolves to the directory's index file, and a double quote inside a single-quoted src injects a second import. All are invented inputs, none is in the corpus. Read and re-run on a scratch clone of a8266bf: red before with the v1.1.0 typescript.mts (fail 7, each failure the issue's own), green after (220 of 220), npm run check green (2762, 2759 pass, 3 skipped, dist fresh). Commits: one per issue plus two corpus commits, dist with src, rev 6 to 9, catalogue 992 to 993 in all three places, no agent trailer. Kept: a <!-- inside a script body or a template string is text, every line and column of the view, every existing Vue answer in the corpus. Merge note: the branch carries release/1.2.0 at 20aceea, the release is at fb89628 (999 cases), so the catalogue count (1000 after the merge) and the extractor-rev test will conflict. (+1 more lines, whole text in the issue's Reviews section)

Every added edge sits on a vi.mock or jest.mock line and reads the mock as the issue intends, so the fix is the goldens and corpus/explained.md for jest, next-intl and react, not code. With the v1.1.0 extractor the same verify gives jest and next-intl ok. Minor (762): yes, a regression against v1.1.0, which skipped nothing: a <!-- outside a comment (an attribute value, the raw text of <style> or <textarea>, a Svelte {@html '<!--'}) that a later --> closes, including i-->0 inside the script, hides every script between them, and the component has no view where v1.1.0 read ./a at line 2. It needs a stray <!-- with a --> after the script, and nothing in the corpus has one (256 Vue views identical to v1.1.0, same length and lines). docs/api.md does not name this limit; name it, or skip <!-- inside a tag's quoted attribute values. Minor (762): the changelog says the change only removes uses, but <!-- <script> --> before a real script gave v1.1.0 no use and now gives the real script's, so it can add uses. Minor (762): the test for a commented script beside a real one is Vue only; the same Svelte case passes on the tip. Minor (763): the src value goes into the view unescaped and unfiltered. A protocol-relative //host/a.js is not dropped as https: is, and a root-absolute /js/a.js in <svelte:head> becomes an unfollowed use although it is a browser URL. A src of .\x.js reads as '.' and resolves to the directory's index file, and a double quote inside a single-quoted src injects a second import. All are invented inputs, none is in the corpus. Read and re-run on a scratch clone of a8266bf: red before with the v1.1.0 typescript.mts (fail 7, each failure the issue's own), green after (220 of 220), npm run check green (2762, 2759 pass, 3 skipped, dist fresh). Commits: one per issue plus two corpus commits, dist with src, rev 6 to 9, catalogue 992 to 993 in all three places, no agent trailer. Kept: a <!-- inside a script body or a template string is text, every line and column of the view, every existing Vue answer in the corpus. Merge note: the branch carries release/1.2.0 at 20aceea, the release is at fb89628 (999 cases), so the catalogue count (1000 after the merge) and the extractor-rev test will conflict. - 2026-11-17 12:46 key 20261117T1032-bhyc by rev-765-opus · 188 review changes · by rev-765-opus (fresh) · model claude-opus-5-5@xhigh · batch 20261117T1246-qtlm · Important 1 (765 line 3, 188's README half): the docs promise a stop past a budget of time or memory that the runtime cannot give on the shapes they cite. README.md:191 ('So a size cap alone does not protect a process with a memory limit ... passes progressCallback ... to stop a parse past its budget of bytes, time or memory'), docs/api.md:148 and 765's changelog entry ('can now end a parse past its budget of bytes, time or memory, where a short repeated text ... takes about 1.5 GiB at 32 KiB') say the callback bounds memory. Traced through the host at 124229c with a callback that never stops: on a<a, at 16 KiB the calls reach the last characters at 103 MiB, then one 265 ms gap with no call, and the next call (at the end) sees 463 MiB; on the C# shape at 16 KiB, 129 MiB, a 782 ms gap, then 1086 MiB. A stop past 200 MiB RSS (TS 16 KiB) fired at the end at 464 MiB, past 300 MiB (C# 16 KiB) at 1099 MiB, a 50 ms stop on C# 16 KiB at 767 ms and 1085 MiB; on TS 32 KiB a stop past 400 MiB fired at 1550 MiB, the unstopped peak, and on TS 64 KiB a 400 MiB or a 100 ms stop did not prevent Aborted() at 2137 MiB. Only a stop by offset before the end works (or a time budget shorter than reading the whole text), and it refuses the file just as a size cap does. On random text (188's shape) calls come at most 7 ms apart (JavaScript and Python, 1 000 000 characters), so a time budget works there. The two paragraphs, 765's changelog entry and 188's Known-limits note must say what holds: the callback bounds the time of text the parser keeps reading; the memory of a short repeated text is spent at its end with no call, so only a size cap (or a process of its own) bounds it. (+8 more lines, whole text in the issue's Reviews section)

- 2026-11-17 12:48 key 20261117T1032-zbnw by w-752-opus · 752 evidence · Assumed: the six npm grammar pins become exact devDependencies as the plan says, and the repository gets a .npmrc with legacy-peer-deps=true: tree-sitter-solidity 1.2.13 misspells its peerDependenciesMeta key, so npm installs the native tree-sitter for it and then refuses the optional peer ranges of zig, scala, groovy and properties (ERESOLVE); with the flag no peer is installed and npm ci passes on the lockfile. The .npmrc is not packed. The lockfile changes by additions only.
- 2026-11-17 12:48 key 20261117T1032-zbnw by w-752-opus · 752 evidence · Assumed: buildGrammars reads the shipped files right after outDir and before every other source (the plan put them before the cache), so an npm pin needs no installed package either; a shipped file that does not match its pin is passed over silently, as a mismatched outDir file is; rebuild passes over them. A new option shippedDir (default the package's grammars/built/) lets the tests of the lower sources point the step at an empty folder, so a checkout that ran npm run grammars:package answers them the same; BuiltGrammar.from gains 'shipped'. Both are in docs/api.md.

Important 2: with a callback set, a runtime abort is retried as a scanner trap (parser.mts:170-175) and rejects as EPARSESTOPPED, and the next parse fails; an abort is never retried and rejects with its own stable code (issue 5, now in this lease). Important 3: currentOffset counts two per character, not bytes; docs/api.md:146 and the README's 1 KiB say what it counts. Important 4: the time figures come from one random text per size and are presented as bounds; other seeds give Python 12.99 s and JavaScript 1.80 to 13.01 s at 1 000 000; give ranges over several seeds or say they are single samples, with no slowest-language claim. Minor 5: options passed third by a JavaScript caller are taken as the language; accept them there or refuse. Minor 6: the stub test asserts the runtime received exactly ['x'].

- 2026-11-17 13:13 key 20261117T1032-zbnw by rev-752-opus · 752 review changes · by rev-752-opus (fresh) · model claude-opus-5-5@xhigh · Line 1 [read] is met by the ruling runes-grammars-in-package: 'Runes ships all 23 built grammars inside its npm package (issue 752): the package grows to about 2-3 MB compressed, each file is still checked against its pinned checksum, the release workflow builds them before publishing, and a user needs no download and no toolchain, Windows included.' Line 2 [test] re-run: green at 9635101 (29 of 29; npm run check 2774 pass, 0 fail, 3 skipped, dist fresh), red on 1f3476b for the right reason. Confirmed: 46 files under grammars/built, 3,526,091 bytes packed, 35.2 MB unpacked, .npmrc not packed (npm 10.9.10, 11.6.2 and 11.19.1 list the same); never in git; a consumer install of the tarball runs no script and gets all 23 from 'shipped' offline; a stray file aborts npm publish (prepublishOnly) and the release pack check; a flipped byte in grammars/built is rewritten to the pinned bytes before the pack; both workflow edits are additions only, no permission or publish step changed. (1) Important, release.yml: the new actions/cache step restores the grammar cache in the one job that holds id-token: write, after check:dist confirmed dist/ and before grammars:package and npm publish run. That job restored no cache before (its setup-node has no cache: npm, unlike every CI job). The comment 'a cache cannot change what is published' holds for the grammar bytes, which are re-hashed, not for the job: actions/cache extracts a tar archive with absolute names, so an entry written by any run in the main branch's or the tag's cache scope (npm ci install scripts in CI, the cold wasm toolchain, the corpus job on the tag) can place files outside the cached path, dist/ included, and they are then published with provenance. This is the cache-poisoning pattern publishing workflows avoid (zizmor's cache-poisoning audit flags a cache restore in a tag-triggered publish job). The run already holds what the job needs: the verify run's grammars job now builds every pin and uploads the grammar-cache artifact, so the publish job can download it and run npm run grammars:package with RUNES_GRAMMAR_OFFLINE=1 and RUNES_GRAMMAR_CACHE pointed at it: no cache, no toolchain, no download, every file still checked against the manifest. Only CI can show that handoff. (2) Minor, .npmrc legacy-peer-deps=true: needed, and today it hides nothing else. Without it npm ci refuses the lockfile (Missing: tree-sitter@0.25.1) and npm install fails ERESOLVE (solidity's misspelled tree_sitter meta pulls tree-sitter 0.25.1, against zig's ^0.22.1 and the ^0.21.1 of groovy, java, properties and scala; no version satisfies all). The only peer edges in the tree are these and the root's optional web-tree-sitter. An override (tree-sitter 0.25.1) resolves without the flag but installs the native tree-sitter binding with its own install script, so it is not narrower. The flag is repository-wide and lasting: a later devDependency's required peer is skipped silently, and npm ls exits 0 with solidity's peer unmet. Narrower shape for the jarl to weigh: the nine npm grammar packages in a folder of their own (own package.json and lockfile, the flag there, --ignore-scripts) used as resolveFrom by grammars:package. (3) Minor: the six packages bring eight with install scripts into every npm ci, the publish job's included: the six, tree-sitter-java 0.23.4 (groovy's dependency) and yarn 1.22.22 (a runtime dependency of solidity, with a preinstall); the record names neither. Runes reads two data files of each. On linux-arm64, zig, groovy and java ship an x86-64 binary under prebuilds/linux-arm64, so npm ci compiles three native addons with node-gyp (a C toolchain needed) and leaves an extraneous node_modules/@tree-sitter-grammars/node-addon-api; a consumer's install from git does the same, as Runes' build script makes npm install the dev tree there. The folder of (2) would remove this too. (4) Minor: 3.5 MB packed against the ruling's 'about 2-3 MB compressed'; docs/api.md and the changelog state 3.5 MB; worth a line beside the ruling. (5) Minor: a shipped file that fails its sha256 is passed over with no log line (a damaged cache entry is logged), and offline the error then names the npm package or the cache, not the damaged installed file (one flipped bit in tree-sitter-go.wasm: 'the npm package tree-sitter-go@0.25.0 is not installed where it is resolved from'). Refused, so safe; only the diagnosis. (6) Minor: docs/api.md and the changelog promise 'no grammar package', but checkRuntimePins, which README tells a consumer to run in its tests, reports each npm grammar package of the given languages as not installed in a consumer installed from the packed tarball; the docs line could say so (the API question is filed outside). Notes, not findings: README:193, :195 and :279 still describe a package without bytes, routed to the final pass by the plan; in npm 11.19's pacote a git install runs only prepare, so prepack would not have run there either, and prepublishOnly is the safer choice anyway. Only CI shows: the Windows and macOS jobs (the six packages' install, the packed test offline), the release steps on a runner, trusted publishing with the added steps. (+1 more lines, whole text in the issue's Reviews section)

Important 1: release.yml restores the grammar cache (actions/cache) in the one job that holds id-token: write, before grammars:package and npm publish: a poisoned cache entry can place files outside the cached path, dist/ included, and they are published with provenance. The publish job takes the grammar bytes from the run's own grammars artifact (or builds them itself) with no cache restore, offline, every file checked against the manifest. Taken in the same round, for the supply chain of the publish job: Minors 2 and 3, the repository-wide legacy-peer-deps and the eight install scripts the six new grammar devDependencies bring into every npm ci (the publish job's included, and native builds on linux-arm64): the nine npm grammar packages move to a folder of their own with its own package.json and lockfile, installed with npm ci --ignore-scripts and the flag there only, used as resolveFrom by grammars:package, so the root keeps no .npmrc and no new devDependency. Minor 5: a shipped file that fails its sha256 is logged as the damaged cache entry is, and the offline error names it. Minor 6: docs/api.md says what checkRuntimePins reports for the npm grammar packages in a consumer installed from the package. Minor 4 (3.5 MB packed against the ruling's 2-3 MB) is noted by the jarl beside the ruling.

Every import of the parent module from a nested module that writes replace <parent> => ../ (the shape of gopls/go.mod in golang.org/x/tools, replace golang.org/x/tools => ../) or => ../../ from two levels down lost its edge against v1.1.0, in the listed view and on disk: a gopls-shaped git repository through the full extractor walk gives golang.org/x/tools/internal/event resolved internal/event/event.go at 1f3476b and 65848ce, absent unfollowed at 95fa115. => ../.. keeps the edge (normalize gives .) and => .. keeps it only because the reader refuses a bare ..; no test or case covers a replacement that names the repository root. Minor (761): a replace with a version on its left is taken whatever version the go.mod requires (replace example.com/data v1.0.0 => ../data with require example.com/data v1.2.0 resolves data/d.go, outside at 65848ce); the toolchain applies it to v1.0.0 only and loads v1.2.0 from the module cache. Rare, and the edge lands in a local copy of the same module. Minor (757): two forms are read where the tool reads otherwise: Poetry's to ({ include = "pkg", from = "lib", to = "target" } gives the root lib, though the package installs as target.pkg), and a setup.cfg key in another case ([options] PACKAGE_DIR = =lib gives lib, though setuptools keeps the case of [options] keys: its parser sets optionxform to str and lowercases only [metadata]). Both are refuse-rather-than-guess candidates. Otherwise the reader took 31 invented forms right: multi-line basic and literal strings hiding a fake table, comment lines and trailing comments in arrays, CRLF, a BOM, quoted keys in headers, keys under an array of tables, nested arrays in a Poetry element, setup.cfg continuation, comment and empty lines, a repeated key, setuptools' first-where rule. Minor (757): with a configured root the project directory stops being a root, as with src/ (the worker's stated assumption), so two answers of a lib-layout project move: below a root manifest (a uv workspace root) its tests package loses tests.helpers (resolved at 65848ce, outside at 95fa115), and scripts/ beside lib/ becomes an ancestor root (import util in scripts/sub/run.py binds scripts/util.py, which Python would not load). Both hold for the same tree under src/ at v1.1.0 already, so the model's gap is filed outside. Minor (739, record): the poetry note is imprecise. The three fixture projects were discovered roots at v1.1.0 already (their root sets are unchanged; the one root set 757 moves in the corpus is tests/fixtures/with-include, now its directory and src/ as its Poetry packages say, and it moves no answer), so import build had three matches and no edge at v1.1.0 too. Judged: an old reading made visible, not a false answer this lease introduced; the true answer is outside (the PyPA build package) and unfollowed at v1.1.0 was as false; ambiguous at least shows the three fixture files. The 30 are 23 uses of build and 7 poetry.x uses in src/poetry/factory.py and src/poetry/poetry.py, split between the real module and src/poetry/poetry.py, which the importing file's own directory offers (one candidate is the importing file itself), also without an edge at v1.1.0. Both filed outside. Airflow judged right: the five files are the src/airflow/__init__.py of airflow-core, task-sdk and the three providers read, each a regular package extending __path__ with pkgutil; which one Python executes for import airflow depends on the sys.path order of the installed distributions, which the repository does not tell (airflow-core's defines DAG and __version__, but the readers cannot know the order), so ambiguous with the five is the honest answer where unfollowed said no file binds. 758: no finding. The fallback drops one name only, only after two or more names, skips an excluded parent like a missing one and never climbs above the repository; the 480 uses that move in click, flask and google-cloud-python are all relative and each resolves to the module its name sits in; edges unchanged; the Python oracle finds 0 disagreements. PHP 739: lookupPhpFqn gives resolvePhpFqn the answers it gave; Slim unchanged. Re-run on scratch clones: red at 1f3476b and at 65848ce for the right reasons on every new case and unit test, green at 95fa115; npm run check exit 0 (2792 tests, 2789 pass, 0 fail, 3 skipped), dist fresh; npm run coverage exit 0 (99.23 lines, 95.91 branches, 99.12 functions); corpus verify ok for click, flask, poetry, airflow, gin, Slim, sh, google-cloud-python; oracles 0 disagreements for click, flask, poetry, airflow (go list skipped: go not installed, open). Conventions: one commit per issue, dist with src, docs/api.md matches the exports, catalogue 1001 to 1006 in all three places, no extractor output changed so no rev bump, no weakened test (go-replace-directive-silence keeps its silence with a corrected rule), no trailer naming an agent. 757 line 2 [read] is met: docs/api.md, Python source roots from configuration (added in 1.2.0): 'They are the directories its configuration names, read from these keys only: in pyproject.toml, [tool.setuptools.packages.find] where (an array of strings), the empty key of [tool.setuptools] package-dir (package-dir = { "" = "lib" }, or the table [tool.setuptools.package-dir]), the from of each element of Poetry's [tool.poetry] packages (an element without from names the project's directory), and the directory of each path of Hatch's [tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel] packages (lib/pkg names lib); in setup.cfg, the empty key of [options] package_dir (package_dir = then = lib) and the first directory of [options.packages.find] where.' - 2026-11-17 13:19 key 20261117T1032-bhyc by w-765-opus · 188 evidence · Supersedes the earlier note with the Known-limits sentence (review round 1, findings 1 and 4). For the final pass, the CHANGELOG [1.2.0] "Known limits" line of 188 and 765 together, exactly: "- A file that is not source code in its language parses in time that grows with its size and varies several-fold from one text to the next (1 000 000 characters of random text took from 0.06 s to more than 120 s over the five languages measured), and a short text repeated over and over parses in memory that grows about fourfold each time the file doubles, spent at the end of the text where no progress callback can stop it (TypeScript `a<a,` repeated takes 1.5 GiB at 32 KiB, and C# `int x = f(a < b, a < b, …` makes the runtime abort at its 2 GiB limit at 32 KiB); a consumer caps the size of the files it reads, skips binaries, passes a `progressCallback` with a time budget, and restarts the process on `ERUNTIMEABORTED`." The numbers are those of README.md's host section on branch jarl/765-parse-stop (web-tree-sitter 0.27.2, the 1.2.0 grammar pins).
- 2026-11-17 13:19 key 20261117T1228-objt by w-765-opus · 5 evidence · Assumed: an abort is told by the runtime's message, which starts with "Aborted(" (emscripten's abort in web-tree-sitter 0.27.2 throws a WebAssembly.RuntimeError so named); the host does not recreate its runtime, because a probe showed a new host over the same runtime failing and a second import of the runtime module crashing the process, so the code tells the consumer to restart the process, and the host refuses every later parse with the same code rather than give trees from a damaged runtime. A throw that is no abort keeps the scanner-trap retry, and the retry's own abort is caught the same way.

Once one indexOf finds no -->, no later <!-- can close; remember that, and add a linear-time test for a component. Minor (762): the regex-literal limit should be named in docs/api.md. The paragraph says a <!-- inside a Svelte {…} expression is text with no exception, and the failure hides the whole component: the view is null where v1.1.0 read ./a, every edge is lost and nothing tells the consumer. Name it with the same limit for a Vue {{ '<!--' }} interpolation, which is not tracked either and also gives null where v1.1.0 read the script (Vue 3 reads <!-- inside an interpolation as text). Both need a <!-- inside the expression and a --> after the script, and none is in the corpus. Minor (762): a self-closing component named like a raw-text element (<Title />, <Textarea />) starts raw text that never ends, so a commented script after it is still read, as v1.1.0 read it. A tag closed by /> should start no raw text. Minor (762): the rewritten changelog gives <!-- <script>…</script> --> followed by a real <script> as the case that was hidden before, but v1.1.0 read that real script. The hidden case is a commented opening tag alone, <!-- <script> -->, before a real script, where v1.1.0 read nothing and the tip reads ./a. Read and re-run on a scratch clone of ffa04f7: the new 762 tests are red at a8266bf (fail 3) and green at the tip (224 of 224); npm run check is green (2784, 2781 pass, 3 skipped, dist fresh). The routine corpus gate passes. All 256 Vue views equal v1.1.0's, with length and lines kept. Fixed: a <!-- in attribute values, style, textarea and title, Svelte braces (nested and in tag expressions), and i-->0 in a script; a commented script beside a real one now passes in Svelte too.

- 2026-11-17 13:41 key 20261117T1032-zbnw by rev-752-opus · 752 review approve · by rev-752-opus (fresh) · model claude-opus-5-5@xhigh · batch 20261117T1341-vzll · 752 line 1 [read] is met by the ruling runes-grammars-in-package: 'Runes ships all 23 built grammars inside its npm package (issue 752): the package grows to about 2-3 MB compressed, each file is still checked against its pinned checksum, the release workflow builds them before publishing, and a user needs no download and no toolchain, Windows included.' (the 3.5 MB measured is noted beside it, note-6). 752 line 2 [test] re-run at 38ef258: npm run check 2779 tests, 2776 pass, 0 fail, 3 skipped, dist fresh, on a clean clone and on one holding grammars/built and grammars/npm/node_modules. 12 line 1 [test]: red at 9635101 (runtime-pins case: go reported not installed though shipped with the pinned bytes) and at 0532eb5 (the packed case: 'every npm grammar the package ships needs no package'), green at 38ef258; in a consumer that ran npm install on the packed tarball, no grammar package: 'runtime pins: every package is the pinned version'; a fake tree-sitter-go 0.24.0 is still reported; a damaged installed solidity wasm no longer stands in. Round 1 findings, each checked: (1) the publish job restores no cache; it downloads the grammar-cache artifact of the run it publishes from (download-artifact with no run-id, after needs: verify, whose grammars job uploads it from the same commit) and builds with RUNES_GRAMMAR_OFFLINE=1 from it; simulated with a copy of the cache: full, 46 files; one pin missing, 'grammar lua is not in the cache ... offline', nothing written; one entry tampered, evicted and refused, nothing written. After check:dist the job runs no third-party code: the artifact lands in runner.temp only, grammars/npm installs with --ignore-scripts from its lockfile, every grammar file is checked against the manifest at the tag, so nothing but this run's build and lockfile-pinned bytes reaches the pack; a second artifact of the same name in the run fails the upload and so the verify. Both workflows against release/1.2.0 remove no line: every existing step and permission is kept; a test holds the order and the absence of actions/cache. (2) No root .npmrc; the root package-lock.json is identical to release/1.2.0's and the root devDependencies are its own; legacy-peer-deps and ignore-scripts live in grammars/npm alone. (3) Root npm ci runs the same four install scripts as release/1.2.0 and compiles nothing on linux-arm64; grammars:package installs 13 packages and runs no script; npm install from git of 38ef258 into an empty project adds only the package, without grammars/npm. grammars/npm cannot reach the package: files names manifest.json, manifest.schema.json, patches and built, and npm 10.9.10, 11.6.2 and 11.19.1 pack 208 files, 46 under grammars/built, none under grammars/npm, the other 162 the same as at 9635101. (5) A damaged shipped file is logged and named in the error that follows ('The package ships this grammar, but ... does not match its pin and was passed over: reinstall the package.'). Commits: one issue each, src and dist together, no agent trailer. Minor: the Publish step's prepublishOnly runs grammars:package again without RUNES_GRAMMAR_OFFLINE and reinstalls grammars/npm from the registry; unreachable today, since every file is already in grammars/built and nothing changes it after the verified step, but the same two env lines on that step would keep the privileged step from any download or source build by construction. Minor, for the final pass: README:199 (the runtime pin check) now misses the shipped-grammar exception, beside :193, :195 and :279 already listed. Only CI shows: the download-artifact handoff from the called workflow under the publish job's explicit permissions, the nested npm ci inside npm publish --provenance on npm 11.6.2, and the packed test's npm ci on Windows and macOS.

- 2026-11-17 13:51 key 20261117T1032-zyax by rev-762-opus · 762 review approve · by rev-762-opus (fresh) · model claude-opus-5-5@high · Minor (762): the new test 'a run of script tags with no closing angle bracket is read in linear time' proves less than its name. The run '<script<script…' is read as one tag name, so it passes in 2 ms. A run of '<script ' or '<script a="' with no > is still quadratic: 256 KB takes 6.3 s, and 3.4 s at v1.1.0, which had the same cost, so that is filed outside the issue. Rename the test or make it hold for the spaced form. Minor (762): a self-closing component named like a raw-text element (<Title />, <Textarea />) starts raw text that never ends, so a commented-out script after it is still read, as at v1.1.0. The fix does not change that, and the limits paragraph does not name it. Read and re-run on a scratch clone of 7823e7e: the never-closing comment now costs one search. The 1 MB component suite is red at ffa04f7 (38364 ms against its 15 s bound) and takes 38 ms at the tip; my own probes are linear at 1 MB and 4 MB for unclosed and closed comments, scripts, braces, raw text, open quotes and tag runs. The limits paragraph in docs/api.md matches probes of each case it names. All 256 corpus Vue views equal v1.1.0's, with lines kept. npm run check is green (2786, 2783 pass, 3 skipped, dist fresh), and the corpus gate passes over the routine tier and react. (+1 more lines, whole text in the issue's Reviews section)

- 2026-11-17 14:37 key 20261117T1057-awxc by w-753-opus · 2 evidence · For the ruling (no code written, stopped for the jarl): the fix needs to know whether a library supertype declares a member type of the name, which Runes cannot see; without it, the only fix is to stop binding a bare name to the package's own type whenever some supertype of an enclosing class lies outside the repository. Counted on the corpus's JVM repositories at 342a1b0 (uses whose group holds a member-type reading of a supertype an import or the source qualifies by a package the repository declares nothing in, and that bind the package's type): javapoet 14, jsoup 39, spring-petclinic 13, moshi 11, okhttp 379, so 456 edges; read by name, none is a member type its library supertype declares (Closeable, Flushable, Iterator, FilterInputStream, SSLSocket, Formatter, JpaRepository, AbstractProcessor, SimpleTypeVisitor8 and the like hold no such type), so 0 false edges against 456 true ones the fix would silence (google/gson has none either: no package of it declares Entry). Recommendation: state it as a known limit in docs/api.md, where the Java and Kotlin same-package paragraph already lists 'a member type inherited from a supertype outside the repository' as one; a sentence can make it concrete (Entry in a class that extends java.util.AbstractMap binds the package's own Entry where one exists; classifyUse answers it as that type) and say why (Runes reads no library's members; the guess would cost the corpus 456 true edges for 0 false ones). A middle way, a list of the JDK's member types of common supertypes (Map.Entry, Thread.State), is the same library knowledge in part, and would need its own upkeep per JDK, as the java.lang list does.

- 2026-11-17 14:42 key 20261117T1032-rptr by w-753-opus · 753 evidence · For the jarl, the plan's risk on 753 (a ruling, not a quiet narrowing): the corpus shows quoted includes of headers the repository does not hold, which the line's rule answers unfollowed. classifyUse tallies at 342a1b0, before (release/1.2.0) -> after: hiredis c absent 224 -> outside 180 + unfollowed 44, cpp absent 3 -> outside 3; leveldb c absent 109 -> outside 104 + unfollowed 5, cpp absent 171 -> outside 139 + unfollowed 32; resolved counts unchanged. Of the unfollowed, hiredis has 41 angle includes of its own headers from examples/ and adapters/ (<hiredis.h>, <async.h>, <adapters/ae.h>, which a build's -I.. names) and 3 quoted: "limits.h" (a system header in quotes), "testhelp.h" and "redismodule.h" (Redis's); leveldb has 37 quoted: 32 "gtest/gtest.h", 1 "gmock/gmock.h", 1 "benchmark/benchmark.h" (third-party, unfollowed under the line) and 3 port/ headers (port_config.h generated by CMake, port_chromium.h of Chromium's build), rightly unfollowed. A narrower rule (a quoted include whose first directory the repository lacks reads outside) would answer gtest outside but also any repository header under a missing generated directory; not done, the line stands and docs/api.md states the rule with the gtest example.
- 2026-11-17 14:42 key 20261117T1032-rptr by w-753-opus · 753 evidence · Assumed: the line leaves three forms open. (1) An include whose name two or more of the roots the resolver searched hold (the probe's root and include/ directories, or a database's roots) answers ambiguous with those files, as a Python module two roots hold does, rather than unfollowed. (2) Without a compilation database an angle include is never bound, but where the repository holds its name at the root, under an include/ directory or beside the includer (the includer itself aside: adapters/ae.h including <ae.h> wraps the library's ae.h), it answers unfollowed, not outside, since a build's -I list would most likely name that header. (3) An include of an absolute path, a quoted include that climbs past the repository root, and a require_relative that climbs past it answer outside; a Ruby constant rooted at a core class, a framework namespace or an undeclared root answers outside as the edge walk reads it (so rack's own Rack:: constants answer outside in rack), and a bare constant whose name a namespace of the repository nests answers unfollowed, since it may be inherited.

- 2026-11-17 15:09 key 20261117T1032-ovqz by rev-700-opus · 700 review approve · by rev-700-opus (fresh) · model claude-opus-5-5@xhigh · Minor (700, reference/relations/rust/rust-edition-2018-src-bench-module-edge.md): with benches/bench.rs added the case no longer tells the editions apart. A resolver that roots a bench at the legacy src/bench.rs in every edition passes it, and under that mutation no Rust case fails at 949d902, where this case failed at the base. Cargo refuses every package in which that edition guard decides anything, so no building fixture can hold it, and the case says so in its Why: this is coverage the build rule costs. Minor (issue 21, not made worse in substance): the six moved probes are named in their Rules, and declaring each file makes rustc refuse it while the resolver answers the same (checked for all six), so those cases stay honest. Six completed cases still keep a probe file that no mod declares and that their Rules do not name, so the build test now calls them built while rustc never reads the lines their Expect is about: rust-crate-path-root-item-of-known-macro-edge (src/api/mod.rs), the four rust-edition-2015-use-crate-root-item cases (src/api/client.rs), and rust-multiline-string-path-dependency-edge (crates/app/src/cmd/run.rs, which builds and keeps both edges once crates/app declares it). rust-lib-path-outside-src-main-module-edge also keeps all of src/cli/run.rs out of the build although rustc refuses only line 2: the declared file builds without that line. Before 700 the build list showed these cases. Now only issue 21 does. Minor (test/helpers/rust-fixtures.mjs importedMacros): a known macro invoked bare goes into the stub of every crate taken with #[macro_use], so the lazy_static and type-macros stubs each define both macros, and a case that takes cfg_if! from an unrelated crate still builds (checked on rust-mod-decl-inside-cfg-if-nested-inline-and-path-edge). The build test cannot catch a macro taken from the wrong crate. Minor (RUSTC_BOOTSTRAP): acceptable on CI. It goes only into the throwaway case directory .cargo/config.toml, only the macros 2.0 case triggers it, and that case says it builds as a nightly compiler reads it. Without it rustc refuses the case with E0554 (checked). The trigger is any #![feature( text in a case source rather than a named case, so a later case could rely on an unstable feature without anyone choosing that. Minor (wording): the Expect comment of rust-inline-bare-root-silence still says the path names a crate the package does not have, which is no longer true. The two lines added to the Rule of rust-use-external-crate-silence are hard-wrapped, continuing a paragraph that already was. Minor (record): the notes say an edge moved to another file in two cases. The diff moves one Expect edge (rust-patch-renamed-package-relative-path-edge); the six moved probes are silence lines. Checked and holding: red at 31d06b1 with 45 broken, green at 949d902 with tests 4 pass 4 skipped 0, npm run check 2779/2776/0/3 with dist fresh. The 41 changed cases plus 4 built by the writer alone, the 14 cases that gained the edges of their added mod lines, no rename and no count change all match. Both autolib cases now fail when the resolver ignores autolib (at the base they passed anyway). Mutations of the patch-relative path, the patch rename and per-target editions fail the same cases at base and tip. The new registry dependencies mask none of the traps (five fixture mutations). The git-source version bump, the two-member split and the cfg(test) import are each needed for Cargo (checked). The six probe moves keep the resolver answer they had where declared. (+1 more lines, whole text in the issue's Reviews section)

[read] 755 line 1 is met by ruling runes-1-2-0-plan, D2: 'the alias is read from the literal forms of Vite, webpack and Babel configuration the plan lists, and a configuration that exists but whose alias cannot be read makes a bare specifier that names no declared dependency unfollowed'. Every [test] line was re-run in a scratch clone: red at 4c47621, 45d8cfe and dba567a, green at 29c0bd4 (npm run check: 2848 tests, 2845 pass, 0 fail, 3 skipped, dist/ fresh; routine corpus gate passed, 25 repositories; react, typeorm, shaka-player passed). The worker's departure from the plan holds: Vite's bundled @rollup/plugin-alias resolves the replaced id with this.resolve(updatedId, importer), and enhanced-resolve's AliasUtils keeps the request's path, so both bundlers resolve a relative replacement from the importing file. The token reader is linear: a 10 MB configuration in 0.8 s, 10 MB of bundle-like text in 0.7 s, 2 MB of nested brackets in 0.2 s. No prototype-property crash was found: in the reader (keys __proto__, constructor, toString, hasOwnProperty), in declaresDependency (Object.hasOwn), or in the mock reading (jest.toString, constructor.mock, jest.mock('a').constructor('b')). The 20 fix gives the same answers, since an opening at or after the last > could never match. IMPORTANT (755, bundler-alias.mts objectEntries and applyBundlerAliases): a member the reader cannot read that comes after a readable key in an alias object can, under JavaScript's rules, replace that key's value, but the reader lets the earlier key decide, which is a false edge. Each of these binds @/x to src/x.ts at 29c0bd4, where Vite binds other/x: alias { '@': '/src', ...{ '@': '/other' } }; { '@': '/src', ['@']: '/other' }; { '@': '/src', '\x40': '/other' }; { '@': '/src', get '@'() { return '/other'; } }; and, one level up, { resolve: { alias: { '@': '/src' }, ...more } } and { resolve: { alias: { '@': '/src' } }, ...overrides }. The reader's own rule for a literal key written twice (it keeps its first place and its last value, so the list is unknown) is the right one, but it is not applied to the members it cannot read. docs/api.md says a computed key or a spread leaves a bare specifier unfollowed, and the unit test 'an object entry it cannot read leaves its key unknown, in its place' pins the opposite. A fix: an unreadable member after a readable key leaves the keys before it unknown (or the whole list), and a spread after alias in resolve, or after resolve in its owner, makes the list unknown. MINOR: (1) a webpack alias key with one * (the wildcard of enhanced-resolve 5, read in its AliasUtils) is read as a literal name, so '@/*': path.resolve(__dirname, 'src/*') never matches @/x, which answers outside instead of unfollowed; it is a false green, not a false edge. (2) module-resolver reads a relative value from opts.cwd, which defaults to process.cwd() (its normalizeOptions.js), not from the configuration's directory. The reading follows the plan, but the Babel case's cite and the worker's assumption call the working directory 'the configuration's'. A nested .babelrc that babelrcRoots lets apply, with Babel run from the root, reads './src' from the root. (3) an alias under any key named resolve is read, so webpack's rule-level module.rules[].resolve.alias, and a module-resolver inside Babel overrides or env, apply to every file under the configuration's directory (both bind @/x to other/x for a file the rule or override does not cover); this is worth a limit line or an unknown reading. (4) path.resolve(__dirname, ...) is read even where the file declares its own __dirname; const __dirname = path.resolve(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url), '../..') still binds from the configuration's directory. (5) bundlerAlias recomputes for every bare-specifier call with no memo: 202037 entries cost 1.25 ms per call, and 131072 alias lists in a 5 MB configuration about 54 ms per call (2000 specifiers in 109 s), because combineAliasAnswers stringifies every list's answer; this is crafted only, and the same shape as the tsconfig paths cost filed outside. (6) 10: Jest documents that jest.resetModules(), jest.useFakeTimers(), jest.enableAutomock() and others return the jest object, but the chain walk follows only the six module calls, so jest.resetModules().mock('x') gives no use and the unit test pins that; the corpus has no instance. (7) commits 87f5310, dba567a, f013917 and 5193854 do not build (TS7022 at typescript.mts:151-152, fixed by 876baf6), so npm run check fails at those commits; the tip is green. (8) the vite and unreadable alias cases sit in the TypeScript matrix's 'JavaScript (no type syntax)' block, not in 'project configuration'. (+3 more lines, whole text in the issue's Reviews section)

- 2026-11-17 15:23 key 20261117T1032-qykp by w-755-opus · 755 evidence · Assumed: an object member that cannot replace a key the lookup reads (a shorthand of another name, such as plugins or extensions) leaves the object readable; a shorthand in an alias object is a key with an unknown value. A directory whose configurations hold more than 64 alias lists is read as unknown (every bare specifier there unfollowed unless declared or built in). Correction of my earlier note: module-resolver reads a relative alias from its working directory (process.cwd() by default), which the reader takes to be the configuration's directory, where Babel runs.

- 2026-11-17 15:29 key 20261117T1529-bqqk by rev-753-opus · filed 29 · Runes, Ruby: in a repository that declares a framework namespace itself (rack's module Rack), the e…
- 2026-11-17 15:29 key 20261117T1057-awxc by rev-753-opus · 2 review changes · by rev-753-opus (fresh) · model claude-opus-5-5@xhigh · batch 20261117T1529-fkzx · Verdict: changes, for three Important findings, one on 753 and two on 3; 2, 736 and 754 meet their lines. Important (753): a Ruby constant under a framework namespace the repository itself declares answers outside. rubyReason returns outside for isRubyExternalConstant before asking whether the repository declares the name, so in rack, which declares module Rack, 1858 uses rooted at Rack answer absent outside at c6ba371, and 1856 of them name a key rack declares (lib/rack/content_length.rb:13 Rack::Utils, declared in lib/rack/utils.rb; lib/rack/deflater.rb:91 Rack::BodyProxy), while a bare Utils inside module Rack answers unfollowed. Line 1 asks that a constant under a namespace the repository declares that binds no file give unfollowed; these point into the repository, so outside is a false answer, and the docs/api.md clause 'so also Rack::Request inside the rack repository itself' states it rather than fixing it. Repro: lib/rack.rb 'module Rack; end', lib/rack/utils.rb declaring Rack::Utils, top.rb with Rack::Utils.escape(1) and Rack::Missing: both answer absent outside. Important (3): a require with a computed argument loses the constant uses inside it that 1.1.0 gave. The new branch in ruby.mts returns after isBareCallTo(node, REQUIRE) whether or not it emitted a literal, so the walk no longer descends into the argument. At 3e125cb, lib/a.rb 'require "#{Shop::ROOT}/x"' gave the edge lib/a.rb:1 to lib/shop.rb, 'require Shop::Cart.path' gave the same edge, and 'require File.join(Shop::Cart.dir, "x")' gave that edge and an answer for File; at c6ba371 none of these uses exists. Line 1 says every existing answer and edge stays as it is (require_relative drops them too, as it did at 1.1.0). Important (3): a require of a file the repository holds outside lib/ and the gemspec roots answers outside, though it does not leave the repository. RSpec puts lib/ and spec/ on the load path, and Rails' test command and the usual Rake::TestTask put test/ there: spec/foo_spec.rb 'require spec_helper' beside spec/spec_helper.rb, test/foo_test.rb 'require test_helper' beside test/test_helper.rb, and the cwd-relative 'require ./lib/foo' with lib/foo.rb all answer absent outside at c6ba371, as do rack's test/cgi/sample_rackup.ru:3 and test/cgi/test.ru:4 'require ../test_request'. These are the commonest requires of RSpec and Minitest test files, and outside tells a consumer to stay quiet about them. The line names outside for a require that binds no file of the repository; the C half of this lease already answers unfollowed for an angle include the repository holds where a -I list would name it, and the same rule here (unfollowed where a file of that name lies under spec/ or test/, or where a ./ or ../ path names a file of the repository from its root) keeps outside for gems and the standard library. Minor (3): every gemspec found anywhere in the repository adds its require_paths to the load path of every file, so a committed fixture gem binds a library require elsewhere: test/fixtures/fakejson/fakejson.gemspec (default lib) with test/fixtures/fakejson/lib/json.rb makes lib/app.rb 'require json' an edge to the fixture. No corpus repository shows it; leaving out gemspecs under test or fixture directories, or naming the limit in docs/api.md, would close it. [read] 2 line 1 is met by ruling java-inherited-same-name-known-limit: 'Issue 2 is stated as a known limit, not fixed: across the corpus's Java and Kotlin repositories 456 edges have its shape (javapoet 14, jsoup 39, spring-petclinic 13, moshi 11, okhttp 379) ... docs/api.md says that such a name binds the package's own type'. A scratch count at c6ba371 gives exactly 14, 39, 13, 11 and 379 (okhttp 370 Kotlin, 9 Java); the rows read (JpaRepository+Owner, Closeable+Parser, FilterInputStream+SoftPool) are true edges, and docs/api.md (f702738) states the limit. [read] 753 line 2 is met: docs/api.md no longer says the reason is left out ('and from 1.2.0 in C, C++ and Ruby, where 1.1.x left it out'; explainMiss 'answers for C, C++ and Ruby from 1.2.0'); its 'says that a plain Ruby require stays unread' is overtaken by issue 3 in this lease, and the docs say what stays unread instead ('A require with a computed argument, a require called on a receiver and load give no use, a load-path entry the code adds itself ... is not read'); the changelog entry names the change, and the quoted include wording matches ruling c-quoted-include-unfollowed. Re-run in scratch clones: red at 3e125cb (tests 288, fail 16, each new test and case for the right reason), green at c6ba371 (919 of 919; npm run check 2838, pass 2835, skipped 3, dist fresh), hiredis checked c 44 to c 50 with 0 explained, corpus:edges ok on six repositories, gson 20 to 6 unfollowed. Every classifyUse answer compared between 3e125cb and c6ba371 on javapoet, jsoup, spring-petclinic, moshi, okhttp, CleanArchitecture, eShop, jellyfin, hiredis, leveldb and rack: 754 moved 16 answers, each a library supertype's member type or a Kotlin TODO() and right, C# none; C, C++ and Ruby answers moved only from a bare absent to a reason, and the only new edge is rack test/cgi/rackup_stub.rb:5 to lib/rack.rb. C angle includes the repository holds were checked by hand (adapters/ae.h <ae.h>, adapters/glib.h <glib.h>, sockcompat.h <poll.h> are right as outside). Conventions hold: one issue per commit, dist with src, catalogue 1013 in all three places, Ruby rev 2 to 3, no agent trailer; the branch merges onto release/1.2.0 at e1f7f23 without conflict. (+1 more lines, whole text in the issue's Reviews section)

- 2026-11-17 15:29 key 20261117T1057-rziq tip by rev-753-opus · 3 tip c6ba3714c5534007611df580ff19d05d54442054
- 2026-11-17 15:29 key 20261117T1032-vdfn by rev-753-opus · 736 review changes · by rev-753-opus (fresh) · model claude-opus-5-5@xhigh · batch 20261117T1529-fkzx · Verdict: changes, for three Important findings, one on 753 and two on 3; 2, 736 and 754 meet their lines. Important (753): a Ruby constant under a framework namespace the repository itself declares answers outside. rubyReason returns outside for isRubyExternalConstant before asking whether the repository declares the name, so in rack, which declares module Rack, 1858 uses rooted at Rack answer absent outside at c6ba371, and 1856 of them name a key rack declares (lib/rack/content_length.rb:13 Rack::Utils, declared in lib/rack/utils.rb; lib/rack/deflater.rb:91 Rack::BodyProxy), while a bare Utils inside module Rack answers unfollowed. Line 1 asks that a constant under a namespace the repository declares that binds no file give unfollowed; these point into the repository, so outside is a false answer, and the docs/api.md clause 'so also Rack::Request inside the rack repository itself' states it rather than fixing it. Repro: lib/rack.rb 'module Rack; end', lib/rack/utils.rb declaring Rack::Utils, top.rb with Rack::Utils.escape(1) and Rack::Missing: both answer absent outside. Important (3): a require with a computed argument loses the constant uses inside it that 1.1.0 gave. The new branch in ruby.mts returns after isBareCallTo(node, REQUIRE) whether or not it emitted a literal, so the walk no longer descends into the argument. At 3e125cb, lib/a.rb 'require "#{Shop::ROOT}/x"' gave the edge lib/a.rb:1 to lib/shop.rb, 'require Shop::Cart.path' gave the same edge, and 'require File.join(Shop::Cart.dir, "x")' gave that edge and an answer for File; at c6ba371 none of these uses exists. Line 1 says every existing answer and edge stays as it is (require_relative drops them too, as it did at 1.1.0). Important (3): a require of a file the repository holds outside lib/ and the gemspec roots answers outside, though it does not leave the repository. RSpec puts lib/ and spec/ on the load path, and Rails' test command and the usual Rake::TestTask put test/ there: spec/foo_spec.rb 'require spec_helper' beside spec/spec_helper.rb, test/foo_test.rb 'require test_helper' beside test/test_helper.rb, and the cwd-relative 'require ./lib/foo' with lib/foo.rb all answer absent outside at c6ba371, as do rack's test/cgi/sample_rackup.ru:3 and test/cgi/test.ru:4 'require ../test_request'. These are the commonest requires of RSpec and Minitest test files, and outside tells a consumer to stay quiet about them. The line names outside for a require that binds no file of the repository; the C half of this lease already answers unfollowed for an angle include the repository holds where a -I list would name it, and the same rule here (unfollowed where a file of that name lies under spec/ or test/, or where a ./ or ../ path names a file of the repository from its root) keeps outside for gems and the standard library. Minor (3): every gemspec found anywhere in the repository adds its require_paths to the load path of every file, so a committed fixture gem binds a library require elsewhere: test/fixtures/fakejson/fakejson.gemspec (default lib) with test/fixtures/fakejson/lib/json.rb makes lib/app.rb 'require json' an edge to the fixture. No corpus repository shows it; leaving out gemspecs under test or fixture directories, or naming the limit in docs/api.md, would close it. A scratch count at c6ba371 gives exactly 14, 39, 13, 11 and 379 (okhttp 370 Kotlin, 9 Java); the rows read (JpaRepository+Owner, Closeable+Parser, FilterInputStream+SoftPool) are true edges, and docs/api.md (f702738) states the limit. - 2026-11-17 15:30 key 20261117T1032-rptr by jarl · 753 round 1 (count 1 of 3) · rev-753-opus. Important (753): Ruby constants the repository itself declares answer outside when their first name is on the list of known external namespaces (Rack, RSpec, Rake, Bundler, Minitest, Sinatra, the Rails frameworks): in rack, 1856 uses such as Rack::Utils answer outside while a bare Utils inside module Rack answers unfollowed; check the repository's declarations before the known-external list, and docs/api.md stops stating the false answer. Important (3): the new require branch returns even when it found no plain string, so constants inside a computed argument (require "#{Shop::ROOT}/x", require Shop::Cart.path) lose the edges they had at v1.1.0. Important (3): a require of a file the repository holds outside lib/ and the gemspec roots (require 'spec_helper' with spec/spec_helper.rb, 'test_helper', './lib/foo', '../test_request') answers outside; it answers unfollowed, as a C header the repository holds does (or binds it where the load path is known: spec/ and test/ for files under them, a ./ or ../ path from the file). Minor (3), taken: a gemspec under a test or fixture directory adds no load path for files outside it.
