- 2026-11-17 15:33 key 20261117T1317-edwx by rev-13-opus · 13 review changes · by rev-13-opus (fresh) · model claude-opus-5-5@xhigh · batch 20261117T1533-thfl · Important (16, with 15): beyond the two answers line 16 names, true edges v1.1.0 bound now answer absent outside, against 'every other answer stays as it is' and under runes-1-2-0-cut-for-new-findings (a regression against v1.1.0, and a false answer): (a) a package importing itself by its absolute name from a directory beside src/ or a configured root, flask tests/test_apps/blueprintapp/__init__.py:5 and :6 and, in an invented tree, docs/_ext/myext/__init__.py importing myext.util; inside package P an import of P.x binds through P's own __path__ whenever the file runs as P at all, so outside is false, not only a lost edge; (b) pytest's prepend basedir of a test module inside packages where that directory is neither a project's directory nor holds conftest.py: beside src/ or lib/, tests/unit/test_a.py with tests/unit/__init__.py and no tests/__init__.py resolves from helpers import make and from unit.common import c at 3e125cb and answers outside at the tip, though pytest inserts tests/, the very rule the change cites for tests.helpers; (c) the same for a test module in a namespace directory below src/ (src/app_tests/test_x.py: import helpers), through 15's own-directory rule. So the flask cost is not acceptable as documented: keep the 322-edge golden and drop the explained entry. The worker's suggested inference (a package that imports itself by absolute name makes the parent of its top-most regular package a root for that import) is sound and narrow and covers (a); (b) and (c) need pytest's own basedir for a test module or conftest.py (the first directory upward without __init__.py), with a case each, red before and green after. Important (17 and 18): Hatch sources is read from [tool.hatch.build] and [tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel] together and the roots are joined, while hatchling's BuilderConfig.sources reads the wheel target's sources alone whenever the target sets them; with build sources [lib] and wheel sources [src], lib/ becomes a root and import yaml in src/pkg/x.py binds lib/yaml.py at the tip (outside at 3e125cb), a false edge from code this release adds, and a refused wheel mapping or wheel sources = [] still lets the build-level root count; the unit test asserts that misread (expects [a] for build [a] with wheel {b = '', c = x}), and the Python oracle of 18 joins the two keys the same way, so it would not report the false edge; read the wheel table's sources alone when it sets them, refusing when it refuses, in the reader and in the oracle, with a case. Minor (16): the changelog and docs/api.md name the lost flask edges but not the classifyUse moves of other answers: a top-level name only a directory inside a project holds now answers outside (flask import celery in examples/celery/src, 10 uses, from unfollowed), and one a member's directory holds answers unfollowed (airflow docs.* in */docs/conf.py, 38 uses, from outside). Minor (14): the rule by directory name takes a real project of the repository under a directory named tests, test or fixtures for a fixture (a uv workspace member at test/: import testkit from app/ bound test/src/testkit/__init__.py at 3e125cb and answers outside at the tip); it is the line's own definition, so state it as a limit in docs/api.md or exempt a project the root workspace lists as a member. What holds: every line re-run on scratch clones of the tip 62092b8 and of each test commit (2ad13f8, c567079, 860aca3, 09a565c, 51c4867, 0307966), each red for the right reason; npm run check green at the tip (2834 tests, 2831 pass, 0 fail, 3 skipped: go list and the two Rust build tests); corpus:edges and corpus:oracles over click, flask, poetry, airflow, google-cloud-python as the worker saw, gin, sh, shaka-player and vitepress unchanged; my own classifyUse tally 3e125cb against the tip matches the worker's moves (poetry 31, flask 14, airflow 38, others 0). 13 and 14 hold as written. 15: the worker's narrow reading of a discovered root (one that is not its project's own directory) is right, since reading every root would take the importing directory from every flat-layout script folder against 'every other answer stays as it is'. Conventions hold: a test commit and a fix commit per issue, dist rebuilt with src, catalogue 1016 in its three places, no extractor rev needed (no uses() output moved), no trailer naming an agent, no test weakened but the flask golden named above. (+1 more lines, whole text in the issue's Reviews section)

- 2026-11-17 15:46 key 20261117T1242-imwm by rev-755-opus · 10 review changes · by rev-755-opus (fresh) · model claude-opus-5-5@xhigh · batch 20261117T1546-jirp · Round 1 at 34a5723, re-examined in a fresh scratch clone. Verdict: changes, for one Important finding on 755; 10 and 20 meet their lines. [read] 755 line 1 is met by ruling runes-1-2-0-plan D2: 'the alias is read from the literal forms of Vite, webpack and Babel configuration the plan lists, and a configuration that exists but whose alias cannot be read makes a bare specifier that names no declared dependency unfollowed'. Red: 4c47621 (fail 4 and 4), 45d8cfe (fail 2), dba567a (76 s against 15 s), 94eaed6 (the round-1 alias tests on the old reader, fail 9 of 29), and 9922169 (fail 2). Green at 34a5723: npm run check (2856 tests, 2853 pass, 0 fail, 3 skipped, dist/ fresh), the routine corpus gate (25 repositories, 0 added, 0 removed), and react, typeorm and shaka-player passed. Every false edge of round 0 is gone: a spread, computed, escaped or getter member after a key, the spreads in resolve and in the config, a webpack rule's resolve, Babel overrides and env, the * key, and a declared __dirname all answer unfollowed. The controls still bind. Only react's fixtures/flight webpack config reads differently in the corpus (whole list unknown, correctly). The 64-list cap: above it every bare specifier is unknown, so an undeclared one answers unfollowed and existing package edges stay; that is lost alias edges, not a false green. No configuration in the corpus has more than one list per file, and a hand-written one with 65 is implausible, so the cap stands. IMPORTANT (755, bundler-alias.mts): this round made the reader and the alias lookup superlinear on crafted input, where round 0 was linear (the class of issue 20). (a) readBundlerAliases calls outermost() for each alias property (and each module-resolver pair), and that walks the whole chain of brackets around it, so the cost is the number of aliases times the nesting depth. A vite.config.ts of 1.07 MB (20000 configs inside 200000 brackets) costs the first bare import 13.8 s at 34a5723, against 0.17 s at 94eaed6. At 0.53 and 0.88 MB it costs 3.5 s and 6.8 s, so it grows with the product; a babel config behaves alike (3.4 s at 1.24 MB). (b) makeAliasTable's lookup slices and hashes the prefix at every / of the specifier, for every list, so one specifier costs lists x segments x its length: a 10 KB specifier of 5000 segments costs 4.2 s under 64 lists (70 ms under one), against 1 ms at 94eaed6. The cache is per specifier, so each crafted import pays again. A fix: compute for every bracket in one pass whether an object encloses it; in lookup, try only the prefixes no longer than the list's longest key. Add a bounded test for each. MINOR: (1) the reader reads the configuration object as written and does not see code after it. config.resolve = other, Object.assign(config, { resolve: other }), Object.assign(config.resolve, other), config['resolve'] = other, merge(literal, require('./local')), mergeConfig(literal, overrides) and withPlugins(literal) all still bind @/x to src/x.ts, a false edge only where that code rebinds the same key. Name it as a limit in docs/api.md, or refuse a config object that is a non-last call argument or is assigned to a name the file uses again. (2) a spread before a key in the config or resolve object (defineConfig({ ...shared, resolve: {...} }), resolve: { ...shared.resolve, alias: {...} }) now leaves the alias unknown, though a member before a key cannot replace it in JavaScript: lost edges, answered unfollowed. (3) a __dirname declared as import __dirname from, const { root: __dirname }, const [__dirname] or catch (__dirname) is not seen as declared (contrived). (4) 10: Jest's own type (jest-environment src/index.ts) and runtime (jest-runtime JestGlobals.ts) return the jest object from isolateModules, autoMockOff, autoMockOn, onGenerateMock and setTimerTickMode too, but the unit test pins jest.isolateModules(f).mock('../data/ai') as no use, and the commit says isolateModules returns no jest object. jest.unstable_mockModule and jest.unstable_unmockModule, the ESM mocks that take a module path, give no use either; the corpus has eight such calls, all in jest's type tests. No prototype-name crash was found in the new code (Map and Set throughout; jest.resetModules().__proto__('x') gives nothing). The 1 MB chain of setup calls reads in 0.5 s.

(a) readBundlerAliases calls outermost() for each alias property (and each module-resolver pair), and that walks the whole chain of brackets around it, so the cost is the number of aliases times the nesting depth. A vite.config.ts of 1.07 MB (20000 configs inside 200000 brackets) costs the first bare import 13.8 s at 34a5723, against 0.17 s at 94eaed6. At 0.53 and 0.88 MB it costs 3.5 s and 6.8 s, so it grows with the product; a babel config behaves alike (3.4 s at 1.24 MB). (b) makeAliasTable's lookup slices and hashes the prefix at every / of the specifier, for every list, so one specifier costs lists x segments x its length: a 10 KB specifier of 5000 segments costs 4.2 s under 64 lists (70 ms under one), against 1 ms at 94eaed6. The cache is per specifier, so each crafted import pays again. A fix: compute for every bracket in one pass whether an object encloses it; in lookup, try only the prefixes no longer than the list's longest key. Add a bounded test for each. MINOR: (1) the reader reads the configuration object as written and does not see code after it. config.resolve = other, Object.assign(config, { resolve: other }), Object.assign(config.resolve, other), config['resolve'] = other, merge(literal, require('./local')), mergeConfig(literal, overrides) and withPlugins(literal) all still bind @/x to src/x.ts, a false edge only where that code rebinds the same key. Name it as a limit in docs/api.md, or refuse a config object that is a non-last call argument or is assigned to a name the file uses again. (2) a spread before a key in the config or resolve object (defineConfig({ ...shared, resolve: {...} }), resolve: { ...shared.resolve, alias: {...} }) now leaves the alias unknown, though a member before a key cannot replace it in JavaScript: lost edges, answered unfollowed. (3) a __dirname declared as import __dirname from, const { root: __dirname }, const [__dirname] or catch (__dirname) is not seen as declared (contrived). (4) 10: Jest's own type (jest-environment src/index.ts) and runtime (jest-runtime JestGlobals.ts) return the jest object from isolateModules, autoMockOff, autoMockOn, onGenerateMock and setTimerTickMode too, but the unit test pins jest.isolateModules(f).mock('../data/ai') as no use, and the commit says isolateModules returns no jest object. jest.unstable_mockModule and jest.unstable_unmockModule, the ESM mocks that take a module path, give no use either; the corpus has eight such calls, all in jest's type tests. No prototype-name crash was found in the new code (Map and Set throughout; jest.resetModules().__proto__('x') gives nothing). The 1 MB chain of setup calls reads in 0.5 s.

- 2026-11-17 16:01 key 20261117T1529-qhru tip by rev-28-opus · 28 tip 659eca8ebbaa6e5d2f29b91edc0e5b6c0f403182
- 2026-11-17 16:01 key 20261117T1057-awxc by rev-753-opus · 2 review changes · by rev-753-opus (fresh) · model claude-opus-5-5@xhigh · batch 20261117T1601-teoy · Verdict on round 1 (tip ecb2e1d): changes, for one Important finding on 753; the three findings of the last review are fixed, and 2, 3, 736 and 754 meet their lines. Important (753): the consequence the worker names is a false answer, and it reaches the default shape of a Rails application. a61fe64 answers unfollowed for any constant whose root the repository declares, a nested reopening included, and Rails' own generator writes 'module ActiveSupport / class TestCase' into test/test_helper.rb from 7.1 on (the templates of v7.1.0 and v8.0.0; v7.0.0 wrote the compact 'class ActiveSupport::TestCase', which the root-anchoring gate keeps outside). In such an application every ActiveSupport constant now answers absent unfollowed: a repository of that test_helper with 'extend ActiveSupport::Concern', 'class Current < ActiveSupport::CurrentAttributes', ActiveSupport::TimeZone, ActiveSupport::Notifications and 'class UserTest < ActiveSupport::TestCase' answered absent outside for each at c6ba371 and answers absent unfollowed at ecb2e1d, though every one of them leaves the repository (TestCase only reopened there), while ActiveRecord::Base, which the application does not reopen, stays outside. A reopening is no definition, as the edge walk and the compact-reopening gate already hold, so these are the gem's constants, and a consumer that reports unfollowed uses would report every concern and every test class of such an application. The rack fix is right and must stay (1858 Rack uses outside to unfollowed, no other answer and no edge moved); the test that tells the two apart has to be narrower than 'the repository declares the root', for example a framework namespace counts as the repository's only where a file on a load-path root of the repository declares it (lib/rack.rb in rack, activesupport/lib under its gemspec in the rails repository), so a reopening in test/test_helper.rb or config/initializers leaves it outside as at c6ba371. Minor (3): a ./ literal binds the file beside the requiring file even where the repository root holds the same name, which Ruby run from the root loads: test/x_test.rb 'require ./helper' binds test/helper.rb beside a root helper.rb; reading it ambiguous where the two differ would keep the rackup case and drop the guess. Minor (3): every spec or test directory on the path counts as a load-path root, also one inside lib/: lib/foo/test/runner.rb 'require json' binds lib/foo/test/json.rb, where only a top spec/ or test/ (of the repository or of a gem in it) is on a test run's load path. Minor (3): any directory named lib, spec or test is a probe place, so a fixture that holds lib/json.rb (spec/fixtures/fake_json/lib/json.rb, or the fixture gem of test/fixtures) turns lib/app.rb 'require json', the standard library, into absent unfollowed; noise, no edge. Minor (3): a ../ path read from the requiring file that names a file the repository does not hold answers outside, where a require_relative of the same path answers unfollowed: rack test/cgi/test.ru:4 and sample_rackup.ru:3 'require ../test_request' name test/test_request.rb, inside the repository. Re-run in a fresh scratch clone at ecb2e1d: red at c6ba371 (the round's tests and cases over its dist: tests 183, fail 9, each for the right reason), npm run check (2861, pass 2858, skipped 3, dist fresh), corpus:edges ok on eight repositories, hiredis checked c 50 with 0 explained, gson 6 unfollowed. Every classifyUse answer compared over 3e125cb, c6ba371 and ecb2e1d on sixteen corpus repositories: from c6ba371 only rack's 1858 Rack uses moved, and the merge of release/1.2.0 at e1f7f23 moved nothing; the computed require argument gives back its edges (require "#{Shop::ROOT}/x" and require Shop::Cart.path bind lib/shop.rb as at 3e125cb); spec_helper and test_helper bind under spec/ and test/; the fixture gemspec no longer binds outside its own directory. [read] 2 line 1 stays met by ruling java-inherited-same-name-known-limit ('Issue 2 is stated as a known limit, not fixed: across the corpus's Java and Kotlin repositories 456 edges have its shape'; recounted at c6ba371, the Java and Kotlin answers unchanged since). [read] 753 line 2 stays met: docs/api.md says the reason is given in C, C++ and Ruby from 1.2.0, the Rack::Request sentence is gone, and the reopening rule is stated ('in an application that reopens module ActiveRecord'), which the Important finding above asks to change. Conventions hold: one issue per commit, dist with src, catalogue 1017 in all three places, no agent trailer.

- 2026-11-17 16:18 key 20261117T1204-odef by w-4-opus · 4 evidence · Assumed: the catalogue cases use a constant the base declares, not a field: a field in a constant pattern is not valid C# (CS0150), and the member condition reads fields and constants alike from the members record, so the field half of the line rides on the same condition. The cases also put the is-type operator x is Order in the derived class, which keeps its edge because C# looks it up as a type first.
- 2026-11-17 16:18 key 20261117T1204-odef by w-4-opus · 4 evidence · Assumed: every pattern form the extractor already reads expression-first (not Order, Order or Line, a parenthesized Order) takes the same member condition, since C# binds them as constant patterns first too; the condition is the receiver's, so a base outside the repository is still not followed (the known limit docs/api.md names).

- 2026-11-17 16:30 key 20261117T1242-imwm by rev-755-opus · 10 review changes · by rev-755-opus (fresh) · model claude-opus-5-5@xhigh · batch 20261117T1630-vabg · Round 2 at a911c51, re-examined in a fresh scratch clone. Verdict: changes, for two Important findings on 755; 10 and 20 meet their lines. [read] 755 line 1 is met by ruling runes-1-2-0-plan D2: 'the alias is read from the literal forms of Vite, webpack and Babel configuration the plan lists, and a configuration that exists but whose alias cannot be read makes a bare specifier that names no declared dependency unfollowed'. Red: 4c47621 (fail 4 and 4), b97015f (the round-2 tests on the reader of 34a5723: fail 3 of 31, 6.8 s against 3 s and 8.4 s against 0.5 s), 45d8cfe (fail 2), 768bd75 (fail 1), and dba567a (73.6 s against 15 s). Green at a911c51: npm run check (2874 tests, 2871 pass, 0 fail, 3 skipped, dist/ fresh), the routine corpus gate (25 repositories, 0 added, 0 removed), and react, typeorm and shaka-player passed. The round-1 time costs are gone: 50000 configs inside 500000 brackets (2.7 MB) cost 313 ms; 200000 spreads 389 ms; 20 specifiers of 40000 segments under 64 lists 26 ms; 202037 keys 931 ms and then 34 ms for 2000 imports. Every earlier false edge still answers unfollowed, and the spread rule holds: before the keys it is read, after them it is unknown, and in the alias object or module-resolver options it is unknown. IMPORTANT 1 (755, makeAliasTable): the trie keeps one node, each with its own Map, for every character of every key, about 200 bytes of heap per key character, so a crafted configuration crashes the consumer. Through makeResolvePathToFile, a vite.config.ts of 1 MB of unique 1000-character alias keys costs the first bare import +214 MB of heap; 4 MB costs +857 MB and 2.1 s; 10 MB aborts the process with 'JavaScript heap out of memory'. At 34a5723 the same 10 MB file costs 533 ms and +12 MB. A fix: keep memory linear in the keys' text, for example a trie compressed along its single-child chains, or whole keys probed only at the prefix lengths some key has; add a bounded test of heap and time on a large table of long keys. IMPORTANT 2 (755, readBundlerAliases): a configuration composed from something the reader cannot read answers outside, where both the ruling and docs/api.md say unfollowed. The reader looks only where an alias property is written, so with no alias token in the file it finds no list and the specifier reads as a package. With vite.base.ts aliasing @ to /src, @/x answers absent outside for each of: export default base; defineConfig({ ...base }); defineConfig({ ...base, plugins: [] }); resolve: base.resolve; resolve: { ...base.resolve }; resolve: { ...base.resolve, dedupe: [] }; mergeConfig(base, defineConfig({ plugins: [] })); webpack's module.exports = merge(common, { mode }), { ...common, mode } and { resolve: common.resolve }; and Babel's { ...require('./babel.shared') } and plugins: require(...).plugins. Yet the same file with a literal alias after the spread answers unfollowed. docs/api.md lists 'a spread in resolve or the configuration object that no key the lookup reads follows' as an alias it cannot follow, and D2 says a configuration whose alias cannot be read gives unfollowed: this is the false green the issue reports, for the composed configurations that webpack-merge and mergeConfig make common. A fix: apply the spread and unread-value rule to the configuration object itself, whether or not an alias is written. A spread with no resolve (and test for Vite, plugins for Babel) after it, a resolve, test or plugins whose value is not a literal, or an export that is not a literal configuration object (an identifier, a merge of another), leaves the file unknown; a fully read configuration with no alias stays as it is. MINOR: (1) the trie is built by code points (for...of) and walked by UTF-16 code units (spec[at]), so a key with a character outside the BMP never matches: '😀': '/src/😀' leaves 😀/x outside instead of binding. (2) 10: unstable_mockModule and unstable_unmockModule return the jest object (Jest's type) but are not in JEST_CHAINED, so jest.unstable_mockModule('a', f).unstable_unmockModule('b') gives only a. No prototype-name crash was found (jest.isolateModules(f).__proto__('x') and the keys __proto__, constructor and toString). 20 is unchanged and still linear (84.7 ms).

A spread with no resolve (and test for Vite, plugins for Babel) after it, a resolve, test or plugins whose value is not a literal, or an export that is not a literal configuration object (an identifier, a merge of another), leaves the file unknown; a fully read configuration with no alias stays as it is. MINOR: (1) the trie is built by code points (for...of) and walked by UTF-16 code units (spec[at]), so a key with a character outside the BMP never matches: '😀': '/src/😀' leaves 😀/x outside instead of binding. (2) 10: unstable_mockModule and unstable_unmockModule return the jest object (Jest's type) but are not in JEST_CHAINED, so jest.unstable_mockModule('a', f).unstable_unmockModule('b') gives only a. No prototype-name crash was found (jest.isolateModules(f).__proto__('x') and the keys __proto__, constructor and toString). 20 is unchanged and still linear (84.7 ms).

IMPORTANT 1 (755, makeAliasTable): the trie keeps one node, each with its own Map, for every character of every key, about 200 bytes of heap per key character, so a crafted configuration crashes the consumer. A fix: keep memory linear in the keys' text, for example a trie compressed along its single-child chains, or whole keys probed only at the prefix lengths some key has; add a bounded test of heap and time on a large table of long keys. IMPORTANT 2 (755, readBundlerAliases): a configuration composed from something the reader cannot read answers outside, where both the ruling and docs/api.md say unfollowed. - 2026-11-17 16:35 key 20261117T1032-mhre by w-709-opus · 709 evidence · Assumed: the outcome is the fix, not a limit. A top-level declaration directly after an annotated_expression whose innermost operand is a parenthesized expression, after an annotation without arguments of its own, takes that statement's first line, with the annotations nested or on several lines. An annotation with arguments followed by an expression (@Suppress("x") (y), @Suppress("x") run()) is a real statement, which the grammar reads so at every depth, and keeps its reading; a test pins both. A spaced @OptIn (X::class) counts too, as the grammar's unsplit reading has it.

- 2026-11-17 16:36 key 20261117T1232-svav by rev-7-opus · 7 review changes · by rev-7-opus (fresh) · model claude-opus-5-5@xhigh · batch 20261117T1636-dydl · Round at 27ec329, re-run in a fresh scratch clone with cargo and rustc 1.99.1 through the docker wrappers. Verdict: changes, for one Important finding on 21; 8 and 9 meet their lines, and 7 meets its line in substance. Important (21): KEPT_OUT_OF_THE_BUILD is exact for a Linux or macOS build only, and the check fails both ways (an unread file not listed, a listed file read). Its CFG_OFF entries name the files of the branch that is off where unix holds, so the Windows job of CI, which runs this build test (issue 28), goes red. With cfg(unix) and cfg(windows) swapped in every Rust case, every case builds and the check fails on 7 files of 5 cases. Also, release/1.2.0 is now at 4922b8b: 659eca8 gives rust-module-in-cfg-if-of-lib-root-item-edge a src/other/mod.rs under the else branch of cfg(unix), so after the merge the check fails on Linux too (reproduced). A file kept out by a target cfg has to be listed per target, or accepted as read or unread wherever its cfg decides, and release/1.2.0 merged in. Minor (7, the line's wording): two clauses of line 1 are not met as written, and both departures are sound. use datacrate::dc; beside a branch's mod datacrate; with that path dependency is refused by rustc 1.99.1 (E0659, reproduced), so only the unit test can hold it; through the resolver it binds src/datacrate.rs and gives no edge to the dependency. imp::g() through the branch's alias gives no use, not resolved src/unix.rs; an alias written outside the macro gives none either, at release/1.2.0 and at the tip. rustc binds it to src/unix.rs, so an answer is missed but none is false, and mod unix; carries the module's edge. The director should reword the line, and file resolving an inline path through a local alias as its own issue if it is wanted. Minor (7): a name a branch binds to a crate now silences that crate's inline paths while the branch's use gives no use itself (32, deferred). After cfg_if! { if #[cfg(any(unix, windows))] { use dep; } }, dep::v() (rustc binds the path dependency dep) answered resolved dep/src/lib.rs at release/1.2.0 and gives no use at the tip, so the file loses its edge to dep. A glob in a branch (pub use self::unix::*) likewise now silences every inline path of its module, a path dependency's included, as the 760 rule for a glob outside the macro does and as the changelog says. v1.1.0 read neither form. Minor (21): the reason given for crates/app/src/cmd/run.rs in rust-inline-package-table-crate-name-edge says only that the build compiles crates/kit alone, but app has no crate root at all. The other sampled reasons are true, and crates/kit/src/lib.rs is rightly a decoy. 21's check can fail (red at a4f4fb4 on 122 files of 86 cases). npm run check: 2845 tests, 0 failed, 3 skipped; the build tests: 6 passed, 0 skipped; dist/ is fresh; ripgrep (480 edges) and react (10801) are unchanged; rev 28 to 30 and catalogue 1009 are in place. (+1 more lines, whole text in the issue's Reviews section)

Important (21): KEPT_OUT_OF_THE_BUILD is exact for a Linux or macOS build only, and the check fails both ways (an unread file not listed, a listed file read). Its CFG_OFF entries name the files of the branch that is off where unix holds, so the Windows job of CI, which runs this build test (issue 28), goes red. With cfg(unix) and cfg(windows) swapped in every Rust case, every case builds and the check fails on 7 files of 5 cases. Also, release/1.2.0 is now at 4922b8b: 659eca8 gives rust-module-in-cfg-if-of-lib-root-item-edge a src/other/mod.rs under the else branch of cfg(unix), so after the merge the check fails on Linux too (reproduced). A file kept out by a target cfg has to be listed per target, or accepted as read or unread wherever its cfg decides, and release/1.2.0 merged in. Minor (7, the line's wording): two clauses of line 1 are not met as written, and both departures are sound. use datacrate::dc; beside a branch's mod datacrate; with that path dependency is refused by rustc 1.99.1 (E0659, reproduced), so only the unit test can hold it; through the resolver it binds src/datacrate.rs and gives no edge to the dependency. imp::g() through the branch's alias gives no use, not resolved src/unix.rs; an alias written outside the macro gives none either, at release/1.2.0 and at the tip. rustc binds it to src/unix.rs, so an answer is missed but none is false, and mod unix; carries the module's edge. The director should reword the line, and file resolving an inline path through a local alias as its own issue if it is wanted. Minor (7): a name a branch binds to a crate now silences that crate's inline paths while the branch's use gives no use itself (32, deferred). After cfg_if! { if #[cfg(any(unix, windows))] { use dep; } }, dep::v() (rustc binds the path dependency dep) answered resolved dep/src/lib.rs at release/1.2.0 and gives no use at the tip, so the file loses its edge to dep. A glob in a branch (pub use self::unix::*) likewise now silences every inline path of its module, a path dependency's included, as the 760 rule for a glob outside the macro does and as the changelog says. v1.1.0 read neither form. Minor (21): the reason given for crates/app/src/cmd/run.rs in rust-inline-package-table-crate-name-edge says only that the build compiles crates/kit alone, but app has no crate root at all. The other sampled reasons are true, and crates/kit/src/lib.rs is rightly a decoy. 21's check can fail (red at a4f4fb4 on 122 files of 86 cases). npm run check: 2845 tests, 0 failed, 3 skipped; the build tests: 6 passed, 0 skipped; dist/ is fresh; ripgrep (480 edges) and react (10801) are unchanged; rev 28 to 30 and catalogue 1009 are in place. (+1 more lines, whole text in the issue's Reviews section)

Important (755): the alias trie keeps one node and Map per key character (~200 B per character): 1 MB of unique 1000-character keys costs +214 MB, 10 MB aborts the process out of heap, where 34a5723 took 0.5 s and +12 MB; keep memory linear in the keys' text (a trie compressed along single-child chains, or whole keys probed only at the prefix lengths some key has), with a bounded heap and time test. Important (755): a configuration composed from something unread answers outside because no alias token is found (export default base, defineConfig({ ...base }), resolve: base.resolve, resolve: { ...base.resolve }, mergeConfig(base, ...), webpack merge(common, ...) and { ...common }, resolve: common.resolve, Babel { ...require(...) } or plugins: require(...).plugins), against D2 and docs/api.md; apply the spread and unread-value rule to the configuration object itself whether or not an alias is written, a fully read configuration with no alias staying as it is. Minors: a key outside the BMP never matches (build and walk the same units); jest.unstable_mockModule and unstable_unmockModule chain.

- 2026-11-17 16:44 key 20261117T1518-dgtb by w-27-opus · 27 evidence · Assumed: when another call aborts while a withParsedFile function runs, that call resolves with the function's result and only the free of its tree is skipped. The function may have read the tree before the abort, even when it is synchronous (the abort can land in the tick between its return and the finally), so rejecting would refuse a sound result; a function that reads the tree after the abort meets the damaged runtime on its own.
- 2026-11-17 16:44 key 20261117T1518-dgtb by w-27-opus · 27 evidence · Assumed: an abort from a tree's delete is reported as 'aborted while freeing the tree of <file>' (later calls: 'earlier, while freeing the tree of <file>, so <other> was not parsed'); an abort from the retry parser's delete or from a reset keeps 'while parsing <file>', since both happen inside that file's parse call. The code ERUNTIMEABORTED and the cause are the same either way.
- 2026-11-17 16:44 key 20261117T1518-dgtb by w-27-opus · 27 evidence · Assumed: the issue's claim that a reset after a stop passes its abort raw no longer holds on release/1.2.0: the reset runs inside run(), inside the guarded parse, so its abort was already recorded (the test of that case was green before the fix). What was wrong there was only the retry parser's delete after it, which the test of a reset abort on the retry parser shows red before. Both tests stay.

- 2026-11-17 16:46 key 20261117T1032-vdfn tip by rev-753-opus · 736 tip 5b8434f7657c10414c132f3c161f24804b769735
- 2026-11-17 16:47 key 20261117T1057-awxc by rev-753-opus · 2 review changes · by rev-753-opus (fresh) · model claude-opus-5-5@xhigh · batch 20261117T1647-hglx · Verdict on round 2 (tip 5b8434f): changes, for one Important finding on 753; the Rails finding of round 1 and the four Minor findings on 3 are fixed, and 2, 3, 736 and 754 meet their lines. Important (753): deciding by the framework root alone brings back, for a whole class of repositories, the false outside of round 0. 9c4ad65 counts a framework namespace as the repository's only where the file of the root's own require name declares it (require 'rack', lib/rack.rb), so a gem that lives under a framework namespace, and a framework whose entry file declares nothing, answer outside for their own constants, which they declare and the edge walk binds nothing to. On four public repositories fetched at their heads (rack/rack-attack 6e417ab, sinatra/sinatra 37de93b, rspec/rspec-core aec5f49, rspec/rspec-rails de70f90), the uses rooted at a framework that answer absent outside at 5b8434f, and whose written name or a prefix of it past the root the repository itself declares, number 205 in rack-attack (Rack::Attack, declared in lib/rack/attack.rb), 413 in sinatra (lib/sinatra/base.rb:1215 Sinatra::Error and Sinatra::Base, declared in lib/sinatra/base.rb; lib/sinatra.rb only requires sinatra/main), 220 in rspec-core (Rakefile:14 RSpec::Core::RakeTask, declared in lib/rspec/core/rake_task.rb; there is no lib/rspec.rb) and 209 in rspec-rails (RSpec::Rails::RailsExampleGroup); at ecb2e1d most of them answered unfollowed. A synthetic gem of lib/rack/attack.rb and lib/rack/attack/throttle.rb answers Rack::Attack and Rack::Attack::Throttle in spec/attack_spec.rb absent outside. Line 1 asks unfollowed for a constant under a namespace the repository declares, and these are the gems of rack-*, rspec-*, minitest-*, sinatra-* and active*-* and Rails engines. The check that 9c4ad65 makes for the root, made for each prefix of the written name, would keep both fixes: the name is the repository's where, for some prefix, the file that prefix's require path loads from the requiring file (rack/attack, sinatra/base, rspec/core/rake_task, the names underscored) declares that prefix; a Rails application's ActiveSupport::TestCase and ActiveRecord::Base, reopened in test/test_helper.rb or config/initializers, have no such file and stay outside. Checked and right at 5b8434f: rack keeps its 1858 Rack uses unfollowed and only its two require '../test_request' moved (outside to unfollowed, now as a require_relative of that path answers); the Rails 7.1 and 8.0 shape (test_helper reopening module ActiveSupport, an initializer reopening module ActiveRecord / class Base) answers Concern, CurrentAttributes, TimeZone, Notifications, TestCase and ActiveRecord::Base outside again; a ./ path both the requiring file's directory and the root hold binds neither and answers unfollowed; lib/foo/test/runner.rb's require 'json' no longer binds lib/foo/test/json.rb; a fixture's lib/json.rb no longer turns require 'json' unfollowed. Re-run in a fresh scratch clone at 5b8434f: red at ecb2e1d (the round's tests and cases over its dist: tests 184, fail 6, each for the right reason), npm run check (2862, pass 2859, skipped 3, dist fresh), corpus:edges ok on eight repositories, hiredis checked c 50 with 0 explained, gson 6 unfollowed; every classifyUse answer compared over 3e125cb, ecb2e1d and 5b8434f on sixteen corpus repositories: from ecb2e1d only the two rack requires moved, and the Java, Kotlin and C# answers are those of round 0. [read] 2 line 1 stays met by ruling java-inherited-same-name-known-limit ('Issue 2 is stated as a known limit, not fixed: across the corpus's Java and Kotlin repositories 456 edges have its shape'). [read] 753 line 2 stays met: docs/api.md gives the reason in C, C++ and Ruby from 1.2.0 and states the framework rule ('only in that framework's own repository, where the file its require name loads declares it'), which the Important finding above asks to widen to the prefixes. Conventions hold: one issue per commit, dist with src, catalogue 1018 in all three places, no agent trailer.

- 2026-11-17 17:10 key 20261117T1032-rptr by w-753b-opus · 753 evidence · Assumed (round 3): a name under a framework namespace is the repository's where, for some prefix of the written name, the file that prefix's require path loads from the requiring file declares that prefix; the path is the names underscored, the namespace also lowercased, joined by / or by - (the gem's own name, which Bundler.require tries first: rspec-rails's lib/rspec-rails.rb declares RSpec::Rails while lib/rspec/rails.rb only requires); names past the root are only underscored (OAuth2 reads o_auth2). The hyphen spelling goes past the slash form the review named: without it rspec-rails moves 82 of its 209, with it 156. Of the review's measure these stay outside at 44ba9f0: rack-attack 2 (Minitest, another gem's), rspec-rails 53 (RSpec::Core::ExampleGroup reopened in spec/spec_helper.rb, Rails::Generators), rspec-core 53 (39 other gems' constants its specs reopen or fake, RSpec::Matchers, RSpec::Mocks, RSpec::Expectations, Minitest, Rake; 14 its own, RSpec::SharedContext, RSpec::MODULES_TO_AUTOLOAD, RSpec::ExampleGroups), sinatra 144 (50 others', Minitest::Test, Rack::Builder; 94 its own, Sinatra::Request, Application, Delegator, NotFound, BadRequest, Error and the like, declared in lib/sinatra/base.rb and lib/sinatra/main.rb, while lib/sinatra.rb declares no Sinatra). Those 108 own names stay outside by choice: no file named after them or after a prefix declares them, and a wider signal (any file under the namespace's directory, or a file that owns another constant, declaring the name) also takes the reopenings a gem or an application writes in such files (a lib/active_record/connection_adapters/x_adapter.rb reopening ActiveRecord::ConnectionAdapters or ActiveRecord::Base), the false unfollowed of round 1. docs/api.md states the limit with Sinatra::Request.

- 2026-11-17 17:13 key 20261117T1518-dgtb by jarl · 27 round 1 (count 1 of 3) · rev-27-opus. Important: the host freeing nothing after an abort only postpones the free: web-tree-sitter 0.27.2 frees every tree and parser on garbage collection unless deleted, so the tree and the retry parser the host leaves are freed later on the damaged runtime outside any handler (a stub freeing as 0.27.2 does: the process exits 1); keep everything the host leaves after an abort, cached parsers included, referenced for the rest of the process, and correct docs/api.md and the source comment. Important (the worker's outside finding, a fault of this release's abort handling under abort-cleanup-in-1-2-0): an abort while the host creates a parser (getParser's first parser of a grammar, the retry's fresh parser, setLanguage) or thrown by withParsedFile's function is passed on raw and not recorded, so a later file is named for it (and the tree is still freed after an abort from the function); record those as a parse abort is. Minor: withParsedFile starts its function when an abort lands between its parse and the call; refuse it there, or docs/api.md says so.

- 2026-11-17 17:20 key 20261117T1441-aqry tip by rev-7-opus · 21 tip 31de543b22345887986aa718da28c6a72898ef28
- 2026-11-17 17:20 key 20261117T1232-svav by rev-7-opus · 7 review changes · by rev-7-opus (fresh) · model claude-opus-5-5@xhigh · batch 20261117T1720-iuss · Round 2 at 31de543, re-run in a fresh scratch clone, with the Rust build tests run on Linux and on a cfg-swapped Windows stand-in (none skipped). Verdict: changes, for one Important finding on 21. 7 meets its reworded line, and 8 and 9 meet theirs (each red before, green after; answers, dist/ and the ripgrep and react corpus unchanged). Important (21): PLATFORM_CFG takes a listed file as read or unread on every platform, so the check can no longer fail, on any platform, for a file of the five cfg cases that no crate root reaches. That is the first clause of the line. Shown: with mod leaf; in the cfg(unix) branch of the inline module deep of rust-mod-decl-inside-cfg-if-nested-inline-and-path-edge made fn leaf() {}, src/deep/leaf.rs is read on no target and the build test still passes 6 of 6. The list also no longer only shrinks for those 12 entries, since a PLATFORM_CFG file read on every platform is never flagged either. And one listed file is already unread on every platform the suite runs on: that case's src/other/mod.rs, the else of cfg(windows) inside the else of cfg(unix), unread on Linux, on macOS (unix) and on the stand-in. Its round 1 reason required it unread; its new reason says rustc reads it on some platforms. The fix keeps strictness both ways: each entry says on which side of cfg(unix) it is read (for example read where process.platform is not win32, or only there), the test requires it unread exactly where its cfg is off, and a file no platform of the suite reads keeps a strict reason, as src/imp/mod.rs does. The other changed reasons are true. Note for the merger: release/1.2.0 has moved to 8e5aa17, and a merge conflicts textually in catalogue.test.mjs (1021 here, 1022 there) and extractor-rev.test.mjs.

A name the repository declares in a file no prefix of it names (Sinatra::Request in lib/sinatra/base.rb, RSpec::SharedContext in rspec-core: 94 and 14 uses) still answers outside; docs/api.md states it as a limit'). The Important finding of round 2 is fixed: on rack/rack-attack 6e417ab, sinatra/sinatra 37de93b, rspec/rspec-core aec5f49 and rspec/rspec-rails de70f90, the framework names each repository declares that answered outside at 5b8434f fall from 205, 413, 220 and 209 to 2, 144, 53 and 53, the 203, 269, 167 and 156 that moved all going to unfollowed. I read what stays by the file that declares it: other gems' constants reopened there, which are right as outside (Minitest::Test and Rack::Builder in sinatra, RSpec::Core::ExampleGroup and Rails::Generators in rspec-rails, rspec-core's fake_libs of rspec-expectations, rspec-mocks and minitest, Minitest::Spec in rack-attack), and exactly the 108 own names the ruling names (sinatra 94: Request 23, Application 19, Delegator 18, NotFound 9, BadRequest 7 and others; rspec-core 14: SharedContext 8, MODULES_TO_AUTOLOAD 3, ExampleGroups 3), stated in docs/api.md. The Rails 7.1 shape stays outside: a synthetic application whose test_helper reopens module ActiveSupport, whose initializer reopens module ActiveRecord / class Base, and which holds lib/minitest/progress_plugin.rb (module Minitest) and lib/rails/generators/thing_generator.rb (module Rails / module Generators), answers ActiveSupport::Concern, CurrentAttributes, TimeZone, Notifications, TestCase, ActiveRecord::Base, Minitest::Test, Rails::Generators::Base and NamedBase outside. A synthetic gem (lib/rack/attack.rb, lib/rack/attack/throttle.rb) answers Rack::Attack, Rack::Attack::Throttle and Rack::Attack::Gone unfollowed and Rack::Utils outside; one of lib/rspec-widgets.rb answers RSpec::Widgets::Gone unfollowed and RSpec::Core::ExampleGroup outside. Re-run in a fresh scratch clone at 44ba9f0: red at 5b8434f (the takeover's unit test and three cases over its dist: tests 128, fail 4, each expected unfollowed and got outside), npm run check (2887, pass 2884, skipped 3, dist fresh), corpus:edges ok on eight repositories, hiredis checked c 50 with 0 explained, gson 6 unfollowed. Every classifyUse answer compared over 3e125cb, release/1.2.0 at b4b3ffe, 5b8434f and 44ba9f0 on seventeen corpus repositories: from 5b8434f nothing moved but flask's 10, which the merged release moves too, so outside this lease's languages the tip answers as the release does; rack keeps its 1858 Rack uses unfollowed and no answer of it moved; against 3e125cb, C, C++ and Ruby answers moved only from a bare absent to a reason, the one new edge is rack test/cgi/rackup_stub.rb:5 to lib/rack.rb, and the Java and Kotlin moves are the 16 right ones of round 0. [read] 2 line 1 is met by ruling java-inherited-same-name-known-limit ('Issue 2 is stated as a known limit, not fixed: across the corpus's Java and Kotlin repositories 456 edges have its shape (javapoet 14, jsoup 39, spring-petclinic 13, moshi 11, okhttp 379)'), recounted at round 0, the Java and Kotlin answers unchanged since. [read] 753 line 2 is met: docs/api.md gives the reason in C, C++ and Ruby from 1.2.0 ('and from 1.2.0 in C, C++ and Ruby, where 1.1.x left it out'), says what a Ruby require leaves unread, states the framework rule and its limit ('a name under a framework's namespace that its repository declares only in a file named after another constant, no prefix of the name having a file of its own (sinatra's Sinatra::Request ...)'), and the changelog entry names the change. Conventions hold: one issue per commit, dist with src, catalogue 1033 in all three places, no agent trailer.

- 2026-11-17 17:28 key 20261117T1032-qykp by w-755b-opus · 755 evidence · Assumed: the configuration is what the file exports (export default, module.exports =, export =, or the whole text of a JSON configuration), read through an object literal, an array of them, defineConfig of one argument, a function whose every return or expression body is one (a function only as the export, an exported array's element or defineConfig's argument, where the bundlers call it), and a name declared once with one, in the returning function or at the top level, and assigned nowhere in the file; parentheses and a TypeScript as or satisfies are passed over. Any other export (an imported name, a merge, any other call, a require, a member, a condition), two exports, or none leaves the file unknown, and a text with no tokens has no alias. A wrapper call that round 2 read through its literal and named a known limit (withPlugins(literal), merge(literal, ...), mergeConfig(literal, overrides)) now reads unknown.
- 2026-11-17 17:28 key 20261117T1032-qykp by w-755b-opus · 755 evidence · Assumed: for each configuration object, whether or not it writes an alias, the keys the lookup reads are resolve and test (Vite), resolve (webpack) and plugins (Babel); a spread they do not follow, one of them that is no literal (written there or bound by such a name), a spread in resolve or test that alias does not follow, a spread in Babel's plugins, or an extends (webpack, Babel) leaves every bare specifier unknown. A Babel plugins array followed by .filter(...) is the array, since a filter only drops plugins. Babel presets and Vite plugins that add an alias of their own are named as a limit in docs/api.md, not read; other keys whose unread value could hide an alias the reader refuses where it sees it (webpack module, Babel overrides and env) are not checked.

- 2026-11-17 17:28 key 20261117T1518-dgtb by w-27-opus · 27 evidence · Assumed (round 2, Minor 3): withParsedFile refuses its function when an abort lands between its parse and the call (ERUNTIMEABORTED, 'was parsed, but its function was not called', the tree kept), rather than only saying so in docs/api.md: the promise is that calls already waiting are refused, and the function would walk the tree on the damaged runtime. This changes the merged test 'a scanner trap is retried before any other call runs': t.rb now rejects instead of resolving 'program', with a message showing it was parsed; its check of the order of the parses is unchanged.
- 2026-11-17 17:28 key 20261117T1518-dgtb by w-27-opus · 27 evidence · Assumed (round 2, Important 1): the list that keeps what a host leaves after an abort is module-level and never emptied, so it outlives the host; at the abort it takes the host's cached parsers and the parsers a scanner trap dropped, which the host now holds until then (a strong reference: a WeakRef could miss one already collected whose free is still pending). An object whose delete itself aborted is not kept: web-tree-sitter 0.27.2 unregisters it from its registry before calling into the runtime. Trees and parsers the consumer holds stay the consumer's; docs/api.md says they meet the same free if let go undeleted after an abort.
- 2026-11-17 17:28 key 20261117T1518-dgtb by w-27-opus · 27 evidence · Assumed (round 2, Important 2): the abort from newParser is named 'while making a new parser' and from getParser 'while getting the parser for <extension>'; inside a parse call, an abort while making its parser or setting its language is 'while parsing <file>', and one thrown by withParsedFile's function 'while working on the tree of <file>'. parseFile's outer catch now records any abort it meets, which would also cover an Aborted( from init or a grammar load, though neither is in scope (issue 35).

- 2026-11-17 17:34 key 20261117T1032-mhre by rev-709-opus · 709 review changes · by rev-709-opus (fresh) · model claude-opus-5-5@xhigh · Important: the new helper reads only the statement directly before the declaration, so two layouts of the same split still declare it below its first annotation, while the commit, the test name ('however its annotations are written'), the helper's doc and the changelog say it lands on the first annotation's line. (a) Stacked annotation lines whose first annotation has arguments: over the 709 text after one filler line, @Suppress("unused") over @OptIn(KspExperimental::class), or @Suppress("unused") over @Throws(IOException::class), split into two sibling annotated_expression statements, and the tip declares primaryConstructor on the second annotation's line (relative line 14 for 13); @Keep over @Suppress("unused") over @OptIn(KspExperimental::class) gives 15 for 13; depths 0, 2 and 3 give the first annotation's line. (b) A comment between the split annotation and the declaration: a trailing comment on the annotation's line (@OptIn(KspExperimental::class) // a reason, the layout okhttp writes as @Flaky // STDOUT logging enabled for test), a line comment, a block comment or a KDoc below it: the previous sibling is the comment, so nothing moves and the base's line stands (14 or 15 for 13). Uses do not move in any of them, and neither layout sits under a tree-read split in moshi or okhttp, so the corpus cannot show it. A fix within the helper: walk back over the preceding siblings while they are comments or annotation-only annotated_expressions, take the earliest annotation's row, and pin (a) and (b) at depths 0, 1 and 2. (+5 more lines, whole text in the issue's Reviews section)

- 2026-11-17 17:46 key 20261117T1242-imwm by rev-755-opus · 10 review approve · by rev-755-opus (fresh) · model claude-opus-5-5@xhigh · batch 20261117T1746-sbwq · Round 3 (the takeover's work) at 909f4b2, re-examined in a fresh scratch clone. Verdict: approve. 755, 10 and 20 meet their lines, and the findings are Minor only. [read] 755 line 1 is met by ruling runes-1-2-0-plan D2: 'the alias is read from the literal forms of Vite, webpack and Babel configuration the plan lists, and a configuration that exists but whose alias cannot be read makes a bare specifier that names no declared dependency unfollowed'. Red: 4c47621 (fail 4 and 4), 328d12d (the round-3 tests on the reader of a911c51: fail 5 of 39, the long-key table at +436.7 MB against 64 MB, and the composed case), 45d8cfe (fail 2), eeecbe3 (fail 1), and dba567a (75 s against 15 s). Green at 909f4b2: npm run check (2905 tests, 2902 pass, 0 fail, 3 skipped, dist/ fresh), the routine corpus gate (25 repositories, 0 added, 0 removed), and react, typeorm and shaka-player passed; catalogue 1026 in all three places. Both Important findings of round 2 are fixed. Memory: 10 MB of unique 1000-character keys costs 330 ms and +9.4 MB of heap, against an out-of-memory abort before; 300000 keys that share prefixes cost +67 MB. Composition: the 13 composed forms of round 2, and mergeConfig(literal, ...), withPlugins(literal), extends in webpack and Babel, and Object.assign({}, base, ...), all answer unfollowed. A fully read configuration with no alias still answers outside. Every earlier false edge still answers unfollowed. The controls bind: defineConfig of a function with block returns, a name declared once, as and satisfies, a webpack array or function, a Babel api function, .babelrc, and a key outside the BMP. Only 13 corpus configurations read differently from round 2, all correctly: 12 jest e2e Babel configs built with Object.assign, and react's extends-only Babel config, now unknown. The export reader stays linear under every crafted input I tried (200000 typed declarations 1 s, 100000 returns 0.4 s, a chain of 100000 names 0.13 s, 100000 nested parentheses 0.09 s, 200000 nested blocks 0.14 s, 2.7 MB of nested configs 0.39 s). Issue 10 chains through unstable_mockModule and unstable_unmockModule (a 1 MB chain in 0.6 s). The open points: (a) an unread webpack module (module: shared, module: { rules } with rules required elsewhere) or an unread Babel overrides or env (a name, a require) can hide a rule's or an override's alias for the files it covers. That is a false edge where it rebinds a key the top-level alias maps (the probe binds @/x to src/x.ts), or a false green where it alone maps the key. Rule-level and override aliases are rare, and refusing every non-literal module would lose most webpack configurations' edges, so it is acceptable as a stated limit. (b) A Vite plugin or Babel preset adding its own alias is a false green (outside), already stated, and acceptable: reading it needs running the plugin. MINOR: (1) the Known limit paragraph of docs/api.md does not yet name (a): add that a module, overrides or env the reader does not read may hold a rule's or an override's alias, which it does not see. (2) more code after the configuration escapes the reader than the limit lists: a destructuring reassignment ({ config } = other), for (config of list), Object.defineProperty(config, 'resolve', ...), Object.assign(config, ...) inside an expression, and a call the config is passed to. All are contrived. Widen the paragraph to any code that changes the configuration after it is written, rather than 'Object.assign as a statement of its own'. (3) a module-resolver pair inside a plugins array bound by a name (const plugins = [['module-resolver', ...]]; return { plugins }) answers unfollowed, though the reader's comment says a value bound by a name declared once is literal: lost edges only.

- 2026-11-17 17:48 key 20261117T1518-dgtb by jarl · 27 round 2 (count 2 of 3) · rev-27-opus round 2. Important: the module-level list keeps every aborted runtime copy alive with its memory (~2 GiB at the limit): Yggdrasil replaces a stopped runtime with a fresh copy in the same process and expects the old one dropped with its memory; two cycles of that at ad5b31f leave rss 4214 MiB with both copies alive (146 MiB at 39e15be); keep what a host leaves for as long as its runtime lives, e.g. a module-level WeakMap keyed on the root class of runtime.Parser holding the list (verified by the examiner: 73 tests pass, replaced copies collected), with a test that pins a replaced copy collectable. Minors taken: an init failing with Aborted( (Emscripten's report of a wasm it cannot fetch) is recorded as a runtime abort, so a later call no longer retries init: record an abort only once the runtime is initialized, and keep createParserHost's doc comment true; wording: a parser from getParser is the cached one the host now keeps, so only newParser's parsers and parseFile's trees meet the runtime's free; say the free can fail (a trap was seen), not abort; a running function that throws its own abort is not told its file was not parsed.

- 2026-11-17 18:19 key 20261117T1551-sqeb by w-31-opus · 31 evidence · Confirmed before any change: v1.1.0 (1f3476b, from git archive, npm ci, npm run grammars, its committed dist) and release/1.2.0 (f64a1ca, committed dist) behave the same, so under ruling runes-1-2-0-cut-for-new-findings the fault moves to the next Runes version and nothing was changed. Probe: a scratch script that parses one generated file with the package's own test host and times rustExtractor.declarations(file) and rustExtractor.uses(file) apart, each run under systemd-run MemoryMax=8G, nice 15, timeout 300. Shapes: bare = N times 'mod a {' then N times '}'; use = 'mod a { use x<i>::Y;' at each level; path = 'mod a { fn f() { x<i>::g(); }' at each level. Throw: declarations() throws RangeError (Maximum call stack size exceeded) at 16000 levels for all three shapes on both versions (v1.1.0 after 286/1046/1223 ms, 1.2.0 after 281/1048/1224 ms), and already at 4000 levels for bare and 8000 for use and path on both. Scaling, declarations at 1000/2000/4000: v1.1.0 bare 56/179/throw ms, use 70/227/864, path 72/267/1029; 1.2.0 bare 54/180/throw, use 68/225/859, path 73/259/1241. Scaling, uses at 1000/2000/4000/8000/16000: v1.1.0 bare 23/78/296/1144/4505 ms, use 36/112/400/1525/6073, path 62/136/483/1941/8670; 1.2.0 bare 28/85/304/1159/4566, use 40/119/418/1551/6080, path 50/151/512/2051/8993. Both versions grow about four times per doubling of the depth (the square) in both functions, and the bare shape, which emits nothing, shows the cost lies in the walk under nested modules itself, not in the names-in-scope lookup of issues 7 and 8. Outputs agree between the versions except the path shape, where v1.1.0 emits no in::x<i>::g edges at all (the in:: reading of a bare path came later in the 1.1.0 to 1.2.0 line) and 1.2.0 emits one per level. Neither part was caused by 1.2.0's changes.

- 2026-11-17 18:41 key 20261117T1032-orqw by w-764-opus · 764 evidence · Assumed: the line is the one an editor shows, counted by LF, CRLF and lone CR, in every language, Go, Ruby and Rust included, whose compilers count lines by LF alone: the issue's reason is a message a person reads and a waiver a person writes in an editor. Ruby's and Go's cases separate statements with semicolons, so they are valid in those languages, where a lone CR is a space.
- 2026-11-17 18:41 key 20261117T1032-orqw by w-764-opus · 764 evidence · Assumed: the helper reads a node's row and column, not its startIndex. On web-tree-sitter 0.27.2 both startIndex and column count UTF-16 code units (a probe with a BOM and two astral characters before an import gave startIndex 6 and column 6, as the JavaScript string counts), but a Kotlin declaration re-parsed alone (parseAt) is padded by row + column, so its startIndex is not the file offset while its row and column are the file's; a test pins that path and the lexical header path.
- 2026-11-17 18:41 key 20261117T1032-orqw by w-764-opus · 764 evidence · Assumed: a case file keeps LF line ends, so the case runner and the C# parity loader read a new fence attribute, eol=cr, that turns the fence's line ends into lone CRs; the schema comment says so. The C and C++ text scan of dead preprocessor groups keeps counting LF rows, as the tree does, so which includes it drops does not change. The C# offsets in declared records (base, members, usings) are positions, not lines, and equal between a lone-CR and an LF file. Rust's exported module and item helpers take the line reader as an optional last argument that defaults to the row plus one, which keeps their callers and tests as they are and keeps the overlap with issue 31 to those lines.

- 2026-11-17 19:32 key 20261117T1032-orqw by rev-764-opus · 764 review changes · by rev-764-opus (fresh) · model claude-opus-5-5@xhigh · Verdict: changes, for one Important finding at an edge the lines do not name. Line 1 [test] (In a file whose lines end with a lone CR, the line of every use in TypeScript is its real line, in a unit test and a catalogue case, red at v1.1.0 (line 1) and green after; files with LF, CRLF, a BOM and U+2028 give the same lines as before · by: npm run check) holds as written: red before 16 of 18, each case on line 1; npm run check green with 3011 tests and dist fresh; the Rust build tests 6 of 6 under rustc 1.99.1; the routine corpus gate passed; declarations and uses over every source file of the routine corpus and seven heavy repositories byte-identical between f64a1ca and b710510 (114 CRLF, 531 BOM, 95 U+2028/2029/0085 files among them, no lone-CR source file). Line 2 [read] (The other extractors take their lines in the same way; each either gets the same case or is named in docs/api.md as counting LF only) holds: all eleven extractors take their lines from lines.mts, no row + 1 is left in src/relations, the C and C++ dead-group scan and the TypeScript ERROR rows still count LF rows as the tree does, and each language has its catalogue case and a sample in lines.test.mjs. Checked and true: every rev moved once (java 8, csharp 50, ruby 4, typescript 11, kotlin 12, rust 31, python 2, go 2, php 3, c 3, cpp 3); 1053 in all three places; the Rust helpers that gained an optional line reader are module exports only, outside the relations index, so docs/api.md and exports.test.mjs rightly stay; C# offsets are positions (with a CR prepended to every corpus file the offset keys move by exactly one and every line by one); a component view keeps its lone CRs; the Kotlin case edited in the fix commit corrects an expectation that never held with LF ends (an imported simple name in an annotation gives no use of its own at either commit), not a weakening; the helper stays linear (10.4 MB TypeScript file of 300000 lines, LF, lone CR and mixed CRLF/CR/LF: 2.4 s each, last use on line 299999); coverage 99.33/96.09/99.30. Important: Kotlin, a use on the row after a nested class header that the view joins to its next-line constructor (constructorJoins, the ktfmt shape `class Options` over `private constructor(...)`) gets a later line in any file that holds a lone CR anywhere, even after it, where 1.1.0 gave the right line. The joined tree is parsed from the rewritten text, whose row there starts at the newline moved after `class`, but lineReader(file.content) adds that column to the file's own row start, so the offset overshoots by the moved name and its blanks and crosses the LF when the use stands that near the end of the row. Repro at b710510 through extractorForLanguage('kotlin'): "package a\nclass Outer {\n  class Options\n  private constructor(val s: Other)\n}\nval z = 1\rval w = 2\n" gives a.Other on line 5; f64a1ca, and the same file with the CR made an LF, give line 4. "package a\n\nclass Outer {\r  val q: Other? = null\n  class Options\n  private constructor(val s: Other)\n}\n" gives line 7, where the editor shows 6. The damaged path reads the same way: its reparsed base and its parseAt chunks take rows and columns of the rewritten text. The plan named Kotlin recovery as a place that needs its own case; no test reaches the joined row. Direction: map a view node through the row starts of the text its tree was parsed from (the rewrite keeps the file's length, so that offset is the file's), then count the file's line ends before it, and pin it with a mixed-ending case on a joined constructor. Minor: commit eee139c adds src/relations/extractors/lines.mts without its dist, so npm run check:dist fails there (dist/relations/extractors/lines.d.mts and lines.mjs untracked); the convention puts dist in the commit that changes src, and the red commit needed no source change. Minor: the new docs/api.md paragraph names Python, Go, Ruby, C and C++ as grammars that can give fewer uses in a lone-CR file, but Kotlin does too, more sharply: its recovery splits declarations at LF alone, so a lone-CR Kotlin file with any ERROR gives no use and only the `*` declaration ("package a\rimport b.B\rclass K { val c: d.C? = null }\rclass L { val c: d.D? = null }\r": nothing, where the LF file gives 3 uses and 4 declarations; the same at f64a1ca). The paragraph should name it; the gap itself is filed outside. (+1 more lines, whole text in the issue's Reviews section)
